SECTION 3OTHER CONSEQUENCES: DAMAGES AND ADMINISTRATIVE
                 SANCTIONS

ARTICLE 33 -- DAMAGES

33.1Liability of the granting authority
The granting authority cannot be held liable for any damage caused to the beneficiaries or to third
parties as a consequence of the implementation of the Agreement, including for gross negligence.
The granting authority cannot be held liable for any damage caused by any of the beneficiaries or
other participants involved in the action, as a consequence of the implementation of the Agreement.

33.2Liability of the beneficiaries
The beneficiaries must compensate the granting authority for any damage it sustains as a result of the
implementation of the action or because the action was not implemented in full compliance with the
Agreement, provided that it was caused by gross negligence or wilful act.
The liability does not extend to indirect or consequential losses or similar damage (such as loss of
profit, loss of revenue or loss of contracts), provided such damage was not caused by wilful act or
by a breach of confidentiality.

ARTICLE 34 -- ADMINISTRATIVE SANCTIONS AND OTHER MEASURES
Nothing in this Agreement may be construed as preventing the adoption of administrative sanctions
(i.e. exclusion from EU award procedures and/or financial penalties) or other public law measures,
in addition or as an alternative to the contractual measures provided under this Agreement (see,

for instance, Articles 135 to 145 EU Financial Regulation 2018/1046 and Articles 4 and 7 of
Regulation 2988/9521).

SECTION 4FORCE MAJEURE

ARTICLE 35 -- FORCE MAJEURE
A party prevented by force majeure from fulfilling its obligations under the Agreement cannot be
considered in breach of them.
`Force majeure' means any situation or event that:

   - prevents either party from fulfilling their obligations under the Agreement,
   - was unforeseeable, exceptional situation and beyond the parties' control,
   - was not due to error or negligence on their part (or on the part of other participants involved

        in the action), and
   - proves to be inevitable in spite of exercising all due diligence.
Any situation constituting force majeure must be formally notified to the other party without delay,
stating the nature, likely duration and foreseeable effects.
The parties must immediately take all the necessary steps to limit any damage due to force majeure
and do their best to resume implementation of the action as soon as possible.

CHAPTER 6FINAL PROVISIONS

ARTICLE 36 -- COMMUNICATION BETWEEN THE PARTIES

36.1Forms and means of communication -- Electronic management
EU grants are managed fully electronically through the EU Funding & Tenders Portal (`Portal').
All communications must be made electronically through the Portal, in accordance with the Portal
Terms and Conditions and using the forms and templates provided there (except if explicitly instructed
otherwise by the granting authority).
Communications must be made in writing and clearly identify the grant agreement (project number
and acronym).
Communications must be made by persons authorised according to the Portal Terms and Conditions.
For naming the authorised persons, each beneficiary must have designated -- before the signature of
this Agreement -- a `legal entity appointed representative (LEAR)'. The role and tasks of the LEAR
are stipulated in their appointment letter (see Portal Terms and Conditions).

If the electronic exchange system is temporarily unavailable, instructions will be given on the Portal.

36.2Date of communication

The sending date for communications made through the Portal will be the date and time of sending,
as indicated by the time logs.

The receiving date for communications made through the Portal will be the date and time the
communication is accessed, as indicated by the time logs. Formal notifications that have not been
accessed within 10 days after sending, will be considered to have been accessed (see Portal Terms
and Conditions).

If a communication is exceptionally made on paper (by e-mail or postal service), general principles
apply (i.e. date of sending/receipt). Formal notifications by registered post with proof of delivery will
be considered to have been received either on the delivery date registered by the postal service or the
deadline for collection at the post office.

If the electronic exchange system is temporarily unavailable, the sending party cannot be considered
in breach of its obligation to send a communication within a specified deadline.

36.3Addresses for communication

The Portal can be accessed via the Europa website.

The address for paper communications to the granting authority (if exceptionally allowed) is the
official mailing address indicated on its website.

For beneficiaries, it is the legal address specified in the Portal Participant Register.

ARTICLE 37 -- INTERPRETATION OF THE AGREEMENT

The provisions in the Data Sheet take precedence over the rest of the Terms and Conditions of the
Agreement.

Annex 5 takes precedence over the Terms and Conditions; the Terms and Conditions take precedence
over the Annexes other than Annex 5.

Annex 2 takes precedence over Annex 1.

ARTICLE 38 -- CALCULATION OF PERIODS AND DEADLINES

In accordance with Regulation No 1182/7122, periods expressed in days, months or years are calculated
from the moment the triggering event occurs.

The day during which that event occurs is not considered as falling within the period.

`Days' means calendar days, not working days.

39.1Conditions

The Agreement may be amended, unless the amendment entails changes to the Agreement which
would call into question the decision awarding the grant or breach the principle of equal treatment
of applicants.

Amendments may be requested by any of the parties.

39.2Procedure

The party requesting an amendment must submit a request for amendment signed directly in the Portal
Amendment tool.

The coordinator submits and receives requests for amendment on behalf of the beneficiaries (see
Annex 3). If a change of coordinator is requested without its agreement, the submission must be done
by another beneficiary (acting on behalf of the other beneficiaries).

The request for amendment must include:

   - the reasons why

   - the appropriate supporting documents and

   - for a change of coordinator without its agreement: the opinion of the coordinator (or proof that
        this opinion has been requested in writing).

The granting authority may request additional information.

If the party receiving the request agrees, it must sign the amendment in the tool within 45 days of
receiving notification (or any additional information the granting authority has requested). If it does
not agree, it must formally notify its disagreement within the same deadline. The deadline may be
extended, if necessary for the assessment of the request. If no notification is received within the
deadline, the request is considered to have been rejected.

An amendment enters into force on the day of the signature of the receiving party.

An amendment takes effect on the date of entry into force or other date specified in the amendment.

ARTICLE 40 -- ACCESSION AND ADDITION OF NEW BENEFICIARIES

40.1Accession of the beneficiaries mentioned in the Preamble

The beneficiaries which are not coordinator must accede to the grant by signing the accession form
(see Annex 3) directly in the Portal Grant Preparation tool, within 30 days after the entry into force
of the Agreement (see Article 44).

They will assume the rights and obligations under the Agreement with effect from the date of its entry
into force (see Article 44).

If a beneficiary does not accede to the grant within the above deadline, the coordinator must -- within
30 days -- request an amendment (see Article 39) to terminate the beneficiary and make any changes

necessary to ensure proper implementation of the action. This does not affect the granting authority's
right to terminate the grant (see Article 32).

40.2Addition of new beneficiaries
In justified cases, the beneficiaries may request the addition of a new beneficiary.
For this purpose, the coordinator must submit a request for amendment in accordance with Article 39.
It must include an accession form (see Annex 3) signed by the new beneficiary directly in the Portal
Amendment tool.
New beneficiaries will assume the rights and obligations under the Agreement with effect from the
date of their accession specified in the accession form (see Annex 3).
Additions are also possible in mono-beneficiary grants.

ARTICLE 41 -- TRANSFER OF THE AGREEMENT
In justified cases, the beneficiary of a mono-beneficiary grant may request the transfer of the grant to
a new beneficiary, provided that this would not call into question the decision awarding the grant or
breach the principle of equal treatment of applicants.
The beneficiary must submit a request for amendment (see Article 39), with

   - the reasons why
   - the accession form (see Annex 3) signed by the new beneficiary directly in the Portal

        Amendment tool and
   - additional supporting documents (if required by the granting authority).
The new beneficiary will assume the rights and obligations under the Agreement with effect from the
date of accession specified in the accession form (see Annex 3).

ARTICLE 42 -- ASSIGNMENTS OF CLAIMS FOR PAYMENT AGAINST THE
                     GRANTING AUTHORITY

The beneficiaries may not assign any of their claims for payment against the granting authority to
any third party, except if expressly approved in writing by the granting authority on the basis of a
reasoned, written request by the coordinator (on behalf of the beneficiary concerned).
If the granting authority has not accepted the assignment or if the terms of it are not observed, the
assignment will have no effect on it.
In no circumstances will an assignment release the beneficiaries from their obligations towards the
granting authority.

The Agreement is governed by the applicable EU law, supplemented if necessary by the law of
Belgium.

Special rules may apply for beneficiaries which are international organisations (if any; see Data Sheet,
Point 5).

43.2Dispute settlement

If a dispute concerns the interpretation, application or validity of the Agreement, the parties must bring
action before the EU General Court -- or, on appeal, the EU Court of Justice -- under Article 272
of the Treaty on the Functioning of the EU (TFEU).

For non-EU beneficiaries (if any), such disputes must be brought before the courts of Brussels,
Belgium -- unless an international agreement provides for the enforceability of EU court judgements.

For beneficiaries with arbitration as special dispute settlement forum (if any; see Data Sheet, Point 5),
the dispute will -- in the absence of an amicable settlement -- be settled in accordance with the Rules
for Arbitration published on the Portal.

If a dispute concerns administrative sanctions, offsetting or an enforceable decision under Article 299
TFEU (see Articles 22 and 34), the beneficiaries must bring action before the General Court -- or, on
appeal, the Court of Justice -- under Article 263 TFEU.

For grants where the granting authority is an EU executive agency (see Preamble), actions against
offsetting and enforceable decisions must be brought against the European Commission (not against
the granting authority; see also Article 22).

ARTICLE 44 -- ENTRY INTO FORCE

The Agreement will enter into force on the day of signature by the granting authority or the
coordinator, depending on which is later.

PROJECT SUMMARY

Project summary

 Grant Preparation (General Information screen) -- Provide an overall description of your project (including context and overall
 objectives, planned activities and main achievements, and expected results and impacts (on target groups, change procedures,
 capacities, innovation etc)). This summary should give readers a clear idea of what your project is about.

 Use the project summary from your proposal.

SUREWAVE will develop and test an innovative concept of Floating Photo-Voltaic (FPV) system consisting of an
external floating breakwater structure acting as a protection against severe wave-wind-current loads on the FPV modules,
allowing increased operational availability and energy output, thus unlocking the massive deployment of Offshore FPV. It
will be focused on the research for securing optimal behaviour at aero & hydrodynamic and structural integrity level of the
external breakwater, the internal FPV modular structure, the connections, the mooring and anchoring and the whole FPV
system, complying with mechanical, electrical (maximizing energy output) and cost-efficiency requirements, ensuring
high lifetime of critical components, high reliability of the system and easy, quick and cost-efficient, construction,
installation and O&M of the whole system.
This will require:
1)the development of novel circular concrete material solutions for the breakwater, cost-effective, easy to produce and
with high mechanical, physical and durability properties and low CO2 footprint.
2)the development of an advanced predictive computational modelling and simulation framework: coupled aero
& hydrodynamic modelling, Structural integrity modelling, material properties modelling and Structural Health
Management to reduce CAPEX and OPEX;
3)the design and implementation of an optimal testing and validation methodology for offshore FPV system to achieve
TRL5, including lab testing, basin-model testing and marine environment testing of critical components assuring high
resilience to corrosion and biofouling.
Maximum advantage of existing expertise will be taken from experts in: offshore wind floating structures, material and
phenomena modelling (SINTEF, MARIN, CEIT), design and installation of FPV in calm waters (SIS), floating solutions
(CLEMENT), circular structural and non-structural materials (ACC) and in social, environmental and economic
assessment (IFEU).

Work package WP1 ­ PROJECT MANAGEMENT & CLUSTERING ACTIVITIES

Work Package Number WP1   Lead Beneficiary  1. SINTEF

Work Package Name    PROJECT MANAGEMENT & CLUSTERING ACTIVITIES

Start Month               1 End Month                                             36

Objectives

Coordinate and organize the project at strategic level, ensuring: project follow-up (project progress control and planning);
continuous update of the project status to the EC; decision making procedures and suitable project administration;
Data management, appropriate Risks management, IPR management and synergy-building and clustering activities with
specific BRIDGE and other EU projects and initiatives.

Description

Task 1.1: Administrative, legal and financial coordination (L: SINTEF; P: ALL; Duration: M1-M36):
A Quality Management Plan will be defined including project procedures (information, reporting, dissemination, etc.).
Simple output indicators will be developed for each WP with the aim to monitor the fulfilment.
Administrative assistance to project partners: SINTEF will act as Project Secretary, assuming: Financial control, review
and submission of progress and continuous reports to the EC.
Project reporting: SINTEF will assume the liaison between project partners and the EC. The consortium will prepare 2
intermediate Progress Reports and 2 official complete Periodic Reports (M1 to M18 and M19 to M36).

Task 1.2: Technical Coordination (L: SINTEF; P: ALL; Duration: M1- M36):
Monitoring meetings: At M1, there will be a Kick-off meeting to review the project schedule, deliverables and
performance. The Technical Committee (TC) will meet at least twice a year, with WPs monthly meetings.
Risk Management: overall risk management: i) Identification of new risks arising; ii) Evaluation of the causes and effects
of the identified risks, iii) Definition of alternative tasks/pathways and iv) continuous risks follow-up.

Task 1.3: Data Management (L: SINTEF; P: ALL; Duration: M1- M36):
Data Management Plan (DMP), including an analysis of the main elements of the data management policy (IEC 62.531):
[1] Data set reference and name; [2] Data set description; [3] Standards and metadata; [4] Data sharing; [5] Archiving
and long-term preservation.

Task 1.4: IPR Management (L: SINTEF; P: ALL; Duration: M1- M36):
The partners will specify, and agree on, clear IPR management rules in a CA to be signed by all. Unclear IP situations
will be identified in the early stages before they create an obstacle for the project. Management of IPR is a key activity
since the proposal preparation.

Task 1.5: Synergy-building and clustering activities (L: SINTEF; P: ALL; Duration: M13- M36):
to build fruitful synergies with: 1) previous and ongoing H2020 results (PV systems, new circular materials, floating
offshore energy systems-structures, structural health modelling and predictive tools), 2) contributing within the activities
of BRIDGE, related to the smart grid integration of the FPV solution as well as related to the business models and citizens
engagement barriers; 3) EU platforms (Solar Power Europe, Ocean Energy Europe), (ETIP Ocean)

Work package WP2 ­ GLOBAL FRAMEWORK DEFINITION & GENERAL
SPECIFICATIONS OF THE FLOATING PV SOLUTION

Work Package Number  WP2  Lead Beneficiary  2. Sunlit Sea
Work Package Name
                     GLOBAL FRAMEWORK DEFINITION & GENERAL SPECIFICATIONS OF THE
Start Month          FLOATING PV SOLUTION

                          1 End Month                                             36

Objectives
WP2 will establish holistic and detailed requirements for feasible FPV installations with breakwaters suited for very high

                                                                                  7
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

wind (speed >25 m/s), current (>1.2 m/s) and wave (height >14 m) loads and corrosion. In addition, the requirements
for installation, O&M and decommissioning are established.
The specific objectives are:
-Establish a technical Stakeholders-Advisory Board (SAB)
-Research, analysis, and preliminary requirements of critical components
-Integration requirements with breakwater and other critical components
-Use-case scenario definition

Description

Task 2.1: Use-case scenario definition (L: SINTEF; P: All; Duration: M1-M4):
Several actual locations, with environmental exposure similar to: very high wind (speed >25 m/s), current (>1.2 m/s)
and wave (height >14 m) loads and corrosion and biofouling will be analysed. Metocean data will be collected and time-
resolved wave spectra will be calculated. The worst-case scenarios for a 20 years return period, in accordance with DNV
RP 0584 (Design, development and operation of floating solar photovoltaic systems) will be defined. In addition, the
data basis for fatigue analysis will also be devised in this task, which is also input to WP3 and 5.
The use-case will also be deeply detailed, including an accurate methodology for calculating the potential power
production, including wave corrected performance ratio.

Task 2.2: Research, analysis, and preliminary requirements of critical components (L: CLEMENT; P: SINTEF, SIS,
CEIT, ACC; Duration: M1-M4):
T2.1 will set the preliminary requirements for the main critical components:
1)Breakwater: A model, based on best practice marine engineering and in extension of already available research results,
will be developed by CLEMENT, indicating the dominating loads on the structure to create a predictable wave spectrum
for the floating installation behind, with the critical collaboration of ACC who will develop the physical solution under
a new circular concrete material solution within WP4.
2)Anchoring/mooring CLEMENT will specify the anchoring and mooring design for an optimal FPV operation in
offshore with the breakwater. In breakwater model, the anchoring technology will be researched considering specific
requirements of targeted use-case locations and based on conceptual anchorages of DNVGL-OS-E301, also considering
the minimization of the use of resources and impact on the environment.
3)Mechanical joints/connection elements The mechanical joints will require a substantial degree of innovation, research
and development by SINTEF, considering the loads subjected on the joints between the individual concrete breakwaters.
The detailed maximum load and fatigue analysis will be carried out in WP6, based on the preliminary requirements and
results from this subtask.
4)Energy connection systems Within this subtask, several mature elements (electrical interface to inverter, transformer
station, DC homeruns from FPV) have been excluded, and the focus will be put on the research and requirements
definition for the junction box, routing and collection of the DC homeruns and the physical interface between floating
solar installation and the breakwater, by SIS with key support of SINTEF.

Task 2.3: Integration requirements with breakwater and other critical components (L: SIS; P: SINTEF, CEIT, ACC,
CLEMENT; Duration: M1-M4):
This task will carry out a general review and definition of specifications, requirements of other disciplines (electric
system, transport, installation, O&M, decommissioning) led by SIS and with key support and advise from the SAB of
T2.4. Specific analysis will be put on:
1)Mechanical joining of the breakwater and FPV array: The interface between breakwater and FPV array will be deeply
analysed and understood. The breakwater will be moored to the anchoring system while the FPV array will be moored
to the breakwater. This task focuses on establishing a knowledge base for designing the surface mooring system. All
implications of on-site deployment of the FPV installation, or off-site assembly of the FPV array will be considered and
the requirements will be defined. For instance, off-site assembly of the FPV array results in strict requirements for tugging
the array to the installation site, where else on-site assembly implies restrictions of the sea state during installation. HSE
requirements, tools, vessels and workload will all be considered in this task.
2)O&M and decommissioning requirements: Exposed offshore extreme locations, puts particular focus on HSE for
O&M crew. This also implies that a significant portion of the day-today O&M must be performed remotely. This task
will set the requirements for O&M, both regarding cleaning, monitoring, predictive maintenance, and repair. The LCoE
of any energy production unit must be calculated including the decommissioning costs and risks. This task will also
define the requirements for the decommissioning procedure.

Task 2.4: Stakeholders Engagement (L: SIS; P: All; Duration: M1-M36):
A technical stakeholder advisory board (SAB) will be shaped, functions defined and finally interviewed to support
with their views, requirements, interests, and specifications to the high interdisciplinary offshore FPV industry. Their

                                                                                                                                                                8
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

input can identify potential show-stoppers and indicate appropriate counter measures. To this end, T2.4 will engage and
have informal advise of: 1) project developers requirements, 2) FPV technology providers, 3) regulatory authorities, 4)
financial institutions, 5) insurance, 6) end-users (energy utilities), 7) installers, 8) O&M entities, and 9) policy-makers.

Work package WP3 ­ DEVELOPMENT OF THE FLOATING PV SOLUTION BASED ON
THE BREAKWATER CONCEPT

Work Package Number WP3  Lead Beneficiary  6. Clement Germany

Work Package Name  DEVELOPMENT OF THE FLOATING PV SOLUTION BASED ON THE
                   BREAKWATER CONCEPT

Start Month              5 End Month                                     16

Objectives

Based in the research and specifications from WP2 (mainly tasks 2.3 and 2.4) of the floating PV system and in direct
interactive loop and coherence with the circular material solutions and the material properties predictive tools developed
respectively in WP4 and WP5, this WP aims to develop the novel concept of PV floating system with external wave
protection "breakwater/damper" made of circular materials.

Description

Task 3.1: Overall design of the internal individual floating PV components (Leader: SIS; Participants: SINTEF,
CLEMENT; Duration: M5-M8):
This task will be focused on the research of the critical challenges of the internal FPV individual components and on the
design of them for optimal operation in offshore conditions. The internal FPV solution aims to be very flat and using
Aluminium individual modules. The main aspects to be analysed in this task are: Global floating PV farm design (to
avoid conductivities, minimize over-wash, minimize splash zone, compensate non-optimum inclination, etc.); Design of
optimal string module (to integrate PV module); Use of recyclable material for the string; Improve corrosion behaviour
of the string; design flexible joints between the floats; Maximizing PV panels cooling; smart sensing of individual floats
´ movements.
In addition, the system robustness and durability will be essential, analysing modules protection, minimization of
electrical connections, accurate design and protection to external environment of the electronics.

Task 3.2: Overall design of the external breakwater (L: CLEMENT; P: SIS, MARIN, ACC; Duration: M5-M10):
As precast elements, they are limited in size and are therefore connected to each other with flexible connections based
on preliminary sizes and geometries of the modular and perimetral breakwaters as shown in Section 1.2.1. Following
the Beaufort scale for wind speeds, the behaviour of floating breakwaters with variable dimensions at real possible wave
spectra in offshore areas will be investigated with regards to the following criteria:
-Wave breaking or attenuating effect (proportion of reflection and transmission ­ critical link from the breakwater
optimization through coupled aero-hydrodynamics in subtask 5.2.3)
-Dynamic behaviour
-Internal load carrying capacity
-Force characteristics and transmission to couplings
To get the most benefit from the system versus the costs, the PV area should be as large as possible. Therefore, all studies
are intended to maximize this area.
The task directly interacts with the necessary anchoring & mooring (T3.3), with the required material properties and
design of breakwater shell and inner core (T4.1 and T4.2) and with the wave loading and overtopping (T5.2).

Task 3.3: Improved mooring line and anchoring system (Leader: CLEMENT; Participants: SIS, ACC; Duration: M9-
M12):
The floating breakwaters must be securely anchored to the offshore site. Environmental effects from wind, waves and
currents acting on the floating breakwaters are transferred to the ground or seabed via the anchorage.
It is therefore necessary to examine how the anchorage must be designed, particularly in terms of economic efficiency,
but in terms of technical feasibility as well.
The anchoring will be either on chains, ropes, or other pre-stressed connections. On the seabed, the mooring can be
designed as a single or multi point mooring. Appropriate advantages and disadvantages will be investigated. As water

                                                                         9
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

depth increases, but also as the size of the floating breakwater increases, cost-effectiveness will be a limiting criterion.
This limit will be determined within the task.
The mooring is also related to the power distribution cables, which are also routed across the seabed and must not be
affected by environmental impacts.

Task 3.4: Development of connection system between breakwater and solar modules (Leader: CLEMENT; Participants:
SINTEF, SIS, ACC; Duration: M10-M12): The floating breakwaters are planned as an outer enclosure of the floating
PV system. Since they are not to be anchored separately and in order to control the interaction between breakwater and
PV system, couplings need to be investigated.
These connections have the essential tasks of keeping the distance between breakwater and PV system constant, limiting
a force transfer between both components and allowing a nearly uniform dynamic floating behaviour.
The basis for the investigations are the results of tasks 3.1 to 3.3.

Task 3.5: Design of coupled breakwater & floating PV and global system design (Leader: SIS; Participants: SINTEF,
CEIT, MARIN, ACC, CLEMENT; Duration: M13-M16):
This task can be divided into two main subtasks:
-Coupling of external breakwater and internal FPV modular structure: A specific analysis to ensure that the array
maintains its position and shape during varying weather conditions: 1) Collapsing the array due to compressive forces
between mooring lines attached to the same anchor point; 2) The full array shifting more than what the tolerances allows
for; 3) Parts of the array moving differently than the rest of the array; 4) Wind, wave and loads due to current accumulating
over a large array causing excessive forces on the mooring points.; 5) Snapping-forces on the mooring attachment lines
and anchors when the full matrix shifts due to changing wind direction.
With the array limitations, the anchoring will be designed for the complex and unusual integration of the offshore FPV
array with the breakwater as a large flexible and floating matrix island, analysing several approaches (e.g. a matrix of
floats that could be pre-tensioned to ensure the geometrical stability of the floating solar array).

-Global system design: The final step of this task will be the definitive global design of the entire coupled FPV
solution including 3D design, bill-of-materials, assembly (individual components, intermediate, global), installation
requirements. It will be developed as a case study for a very exposed location, where the wind, wave and direction of
currents varies. It will consider the definitive concrete breakwater design of Task 4.4 into the global FPV system. This
task will feed the WP7 to set the project solution scenario (mass-energy balance, etc.).

Work package WP4 ­ NEW CIRCULAR MATERIAL SOLUTIONS

Work Package Number WP4  Lead Beneficiary           5. ACCIONA

Work Package Name  NEW CIRCULAR MATERIAL SOLUTIONS

Start Month              5 End Month                            20

Objectives

Based on preliminary specifications on the final breakwater (WP2) and global floating PV design of WP3, the main goal
of this WP is to develop a set of new circular material solutions for the offshore floating PV breakwater whilst ensuring
structural integrity and durability considering very high wind (speed >25 m/s), current (>1.2 m/s) and wave (height >14
m) loads and corrosion. The specific objectives are:
-Design of a set of new circular material solutions for the concrete structural shell.
-Design of a set of new circular material solutions for the inorganic inner core.
-Analysis and selection of the optimal solution of circular concrete breakwater attending to the different combinative
shell-inner core alternative options.
-Prototyping of the circular breakwater, testing and validation at laboratory level.

Description

Task 4.1 Research and design of new circular material solutions for the structural shell of the breakwater (Leader: ACC;
Participants: SINTEF, SIS, CLEMENT; Duration: M5-M15):
This task consists of the formulation of a set of new circular material solutions for the structural shell of the breakwater.
The new circular mix designs will be based on low CO2 structural concrete formulations. Two different approaches
will be followed: 1) Ultra High Performance Fiber Reinforced Concrete (UHPFRC) and 2) Light weight aggregates

                                                                10
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

concrete (LWAC). All these concrete formulations will be designed aiming to reduce the CO2 footprint of concrete by
means of using recycled aggregates and replacing cement with granulated blast furnace slag (GBFS) or fly ash, as well
as to boost corrosion and biofouling resistance. In addition, to boost their durability, these materials will have the ability
to self-heal and seal cracks during their lifetime thanks to its own autogenous healing capacity and to the addition of
crystalline waterproofing admixtures. The circular concrete solutions developed for the structural shell will be tested on
their mechanical, physical and durability properties. This includes compressive strength, density, depth of penetration
of water under pressure, water absorption (RILEM tube), chloride migration coefficient, electrical resistivity, corrosion
potential (Ecorr), carbonation, biofouling, self-healing and abrasion resistance.

Task 4.2: Research and design of new circular material solutions for the inner core of the breakwater (Leader: ACC;
Participants: SINTEF, SIS, CLEMENT; Duration: M5-M15):
In this task, new circular material solutions will be developed for the inner core. All these solutions will be based on non-
structural cellular lightweight concrete. Special focus will be put on developing low CO2 and waterproof lightweight
inorganic formulations, including in the formulation waste materials such as fly ash and slags and waterproofing agents.
The circular lightweight solutions developed for the inner core will be tested on their mechanical, physical and durability
properties. This includes compressive strength, density, depth of penetration of water under pressure and biofouling.

Task 4.3: Durability assessment of the interface of shell & inner core (Leader: SINTEF; Participants: SIS, CEIT, ACC,
CLEMENT); Duration: M12-M18):
The aim of this task is to test at laboratory level the mechanical performance and the durability properties of the interface
between the outer shell and the inner core, which will be critical to assure later on the mechanical integrity of the
breakwater component to be developed in the Task 4.4. The suggested tests are thermal cycles; freeze-thaw cycles;
dynamic flexural loads (wave loads), impact loads and pull off test. The interphase must withstand this harsh lab tests to
guaranty the durability and stability of the component. This task will be developed in an iterative way with the previous
ones in case some materials properties should be optimized to improve the adhesion between both materials.

Task 4.4: Floating concrete breakwater solution and prototyping (Leader: ACC; Participants: SINTEF, SIS, CLEMENT);
Duration: M18-M20):
This task intends to integrate the shell and the inner core to develop a prototype of the breakwater. The first step will be to
analyse and select the optimal solution of circular concrete breakwater attending to the different combinative alternative
options of structural shell solutions with the inner core solutions. Then, a small prototype of the breakwater will be cast
and preliminary tested at laboratory level to determine its density, mechanical performance and integrity.
Once this prototype will be validated at this level, it will be tested at harsh environmental conditions in T6.5.

Work package WP5 ­ PREDICTIVE MODELLING AND SIMULATION TOOLS FOR
MATERIAL PROPERTIES AND STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY MANAGEMENT

Work Package Number WP5  Lead Beneficiary  3. Ceit

Work Package Name  PREDICTIVE MODELLING AND SIMULATION TOOLS FOR MATERIAL
                   PROPERTIES AND STRUCTURAL INTEGRITY MANAGEMENT

Start Month              6 End Month                                       34

Objectives

This WP aims to develop a set of complementary computational modelling tools to:
(1)predict the material properties of the novel materials developed in WP4;
(2)optimize the aero-hydrodynamic design of the floating PV solution developed in WP3;
(3)ensure the structural integrity of the PV solution and its components. In addition, a Structural Health Management
System (SHMS) will be developed in the form of easy-to-use APP to ensure and monitor the structural integrity of the
PV solution through its life in service.

Description

Task 5.1: Concrete materials properties modelling (Leader: SINTEF; Participants: ACC; Duration: M6-M24):
An efficient empirical-based model will be developed to predict the concrete mechanical properties by using ANN Model
Order Reduction techniques. It will be fed with a parametric study from a combination of experimental data of laboratory
mixing and testing from WP4 (composition and mechanical strength) and the in-house numerical tool developed by

                                                                           11
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

SINTEF for LWA concrete to generate sufficient training data amount (if experimental data are lacking). Main foreseen
parameters are binder composition (addition of GGBFS and fly ash class F to the cement binder), the current LWA
types (glass wastes) the current natural aggregate to LWA ratio (in agreement with task 4.1 of WP4) and the presence
of reinforcement fibres as substitute for aggregates (Ultra High Performance Concrete). The prediction capabilities of
the tool will be validated by specific tests in task 4.5.

Task 5.2: Coupled aero-hydrodynamic analysis and design optimization (Leader: MARIN; Participants: CEIT, SINTEF;
Duration: M6-M18):
Subtask 5.2.1 Aerodynamic analysis: Aerodynamic analysis of the breakwater, immersed in the marine atmospheric
boundary layer (MABL), will be carried for relevant inflow conditions (wind speed, direction, and turbulence intensity)
including extreme conditions. The Large eddy simulation models will be employed to capture the flow separation and
turbulence behind the breakwater. A full scale 3D solver from OpenFOAM® will be employed with appropriate sub-grid
stress models. Wind-wave interaction model developed during the ongoing H2020 project UPWARDS will be utilized to
accurately predict the MABL. The aerodynamic loads will be given as input to T5.3 for Structural integrity assessment
for the breakwater. Finally, an aerodynamic assessment of the complete system will be performed for extreme wind
conditions to verify the design.
Subtask 5.2.2 Hydrodynamic analysis: The effect and character of wave-related hydrodynamic loads due is strongly
related to the wave height. Two regimes will be distinguished:
1.Severe sea states. These sea states are not occurring often but the waves have the potential to damage the PV solution
directly, for example by waves overtopping the breakwater and water falling down on to the solar panel modules. It
will be investigated using high-fidelity CFD simulations. A selection of worst-case scenarios will be considered. The
effect of current will be investigated and the interaction between current shear and extreme waves. Validation of these
simulations will be provided by the experiments in WP6.
2.Mild and moderate sea states with a high frequency of occurrence. The loads on the PV system are smaller but due to
the nearly permanent loading fatigue can occur in the mooring and PV module connection system. It will be investigated
using low-fidelity fast and efficient linear methods able to research a wide range of sea states. It allow to model the
entire PV system layout in such simulations. Results include the wave- and wind-induced loads onto the breakwater
and PV modules as well as the loads on mooring lines and PV module interconnectors, which can be used as input to
a fatigue analysis.
Subtask 5.2.3 Coupled aero-hydrodynamic analysis and design optimization Using the aero/hydrodynamic analysis in
subtask 5.2.1/5.2.2 a design optimization step can be performed. The CFD simulations can be used to determine the
required gap between the breakwater and the solar panel modules in worst-case scenarios. The low-fidelity simulations
provide the wave field inside the area protected by the breakwater (see Figure 1 8). The FPV and breakwater configuration
will be automatically optimised in terms of inter-connector and mooring lines loads and material costs through genetic
algorithm-based optimization techniques.

Task 5.3: Structural integrity assessment (Leader: CEIT; Participants: MARIN, SINTEF, ACC; Duration: M12-M24):
The main objective of this task is to ensure the safe, reliable and durable operation of the critical parts of the structure
via the application of fracture, fatigue and structural integrity solutions.
Subtask 5.3.1 Identification of the critical parts/components of the structure A set of finite element analyses (FEA) will
be performed to ensure the structural integrity of the entire structure and to identify its most critical parts taking as input
the geometries, boundary conditions and loading conditions of Task 5.2, as well as the material properties predicted in
Task 5.1. Based on these analyses, some critical components (e.g. the breakwater, the mooring, mechanical joints) will
be selected for a more strict structural integrity assessment (Subtask 5.3.2).
Subtask 5.3.2 Advanced Fracture Mechanics The structural integrity of the critical components selected in Subtask 5.3.1
will be assessed by means of analytical, numerical or/and advanced finite element techniques. For this purpose, the
specifications defined in WP2 and the guidelines of sectorial standards (such as BS7910) will be followed. Both strength
and fatigue analyses will be performed taking into account corrosion and environmental effects. For this purpose, a sub-
modelling methodology would be used. This methodology is based on obtaining a global solution (Subtask 5.3.1) using
a coarse finite element mesh.
Subtask 5.3.3 Definition of the structural monitoring strategy In this task, the location and monitoring techniques
(accelerometers, strain gauges, displacement sensors, corrosion sensors, etc.) to monitor the structural health of the
components selected in Subtask 5.3.1 will be defined according to the results obtained in Subtask 5.3.2.

Task 5.4: Structural Health Management System (L: CEIT; Participants: MARIN, SIS; Duration: M6-M32):
to develop and implement a Structural Health Management System (SHMS) in the form of easy-to-use APP to evaluate
the structural integrity and reliability of the PV solution in service. The SHMS will enable to predict the remaining fatigue
life of critical components and to establish inspection, maintenance and replacement periods by combining structural
monitoring, corrosion management and fatigue assessments.
Subtask 5.4.1 Development of fatigue crack propagation algorithms Fatigue crack propagation algorithms for simplified

                                                                                                                                                              12
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

2D and 3D crack fronts will be developed and implemented. After selection of the crack geometry (elliptic or portion
of ellipse), stress intensity factors (SIFs) will be calculated at various points of the crack front using the approximate
solutions generated from finite elements (Task 5.3). Using appropriate propagation laws (Paris law or similar), the
incremental propagation at each point of the crack front will be evaluated. The resulting new crack front will be adjusted
to a geometry for which FIT solutions are available.
Subtask 5.4.2 Translation tool for finite elements results Development of a tool that translates the tensional results
obtained by means of finite elements (T5.3) to the geometries and crack configurations of the approximate calculations
(subtasks 5.4.1 and 5.4.3). This module will allow the incorporation of new finite element calculations (design
modifications, re-assessment, etc.) to the SHMS, as well as its use by non-expert users.
Subtask 5.4.3 Development and implementation of the SHMS. The components developed in subtasks 5.4.1 and 5.4.2
will be integrated into the SHMS in the form of easy-to-use APP to manage structural integrity and reliability of the
FPV solution through its life in service. In addition, data generated in targeted test campaigns (WP4 and WP6) and in-
service monitored data will be seamlessly stored in the database of the system to feed fatigue propagation analyses and
to manage corrosion.

Task 5.5: Comparative validation of the predictive computational tools (Leader: CEIT; Participants: MARIN, SINTEF;
Duration: M30-M34): The main scope of this task is to verify, calibrate and validate the developed modelling and
simulation tools using the data produced by targeted validation campaigns (WP4 and WP6), as well as with data collected
from literature, standards and available third-party databases.

Work package WP6 ­ TECHNICAL VALIDATION OF THE FLOATING PV SOLUTION

Work Package Number WP6  Lead Beneficiary  1. SINTEF

Work Package Name  TECHNICAL VALIDATION OF THE FLOATING PV SOLUTION

Start Month              13 End Month                                36

Objectives

WP6 will perform the technical validation of the global FPV solution developed in WP3, the breakwater of WP4 and
the modelling tools developed in WP5, as well as system installability and O&M. It includes:
-Accurate instrumentation and monitoring of the different tests
-Lab testing (through advanced hydrodynamic test bench facilities of MARIN and structural laboratory at SINTEF) to
assess the behaviour of the full-scale critical components (Mainly the joints between the different components of the
floating PV system), testing ultimate strength, fatigue/Life-time.
-Sea-exposed durability validation of the concrete breakwater prototype for long term degradation, considering harsh
conditions: high wind, high current, high wave loads, corrosion, biofouling.
-Scale model test validation of the integrated break water and solar modules system in basin.

Description

Task 6.1: Advanced sensing & monitoring solutions for basin tests and concrete component (L: CEIT; P: SINTEF, SIS,
ACC, MARIN; Duration: M15-M24):
T6.1 will define and implement the sensing and monitoring strategy to instrument the FPV basin-scale model (T6.4) and
the breakwater prototype (T6.5) taking into account a wireless data acquisition. The sensors will enable to gather data
during the testing phase, which will be used to validate the models and structural management system developed in WP5.
The location of the sensors and the monitoring techniques to be used will be key for this task. The monitoring system
will be capable of detecting failures on critical points of the structure so that the designer can optimize the properties
of the material (WP4) and/or the geometry (WP3) of the structure for future improved designs. Parameters that should
be monitored include:
-Elevations, motion, accelerations, wave loads for the model test performed at MARIN (for task 6.4)
-Long term Biofouling deposition and corrosion in rebars on the concrete breakwater in harbor (for task 6.5)

Task 6.2: Critical connections´ ultimate strength performances (Leader: SINTEF; Participants: SIS, CEIT, CLEMENT,
ACC; Duration: M16-M22):
The aim of the task is to assess the ultimate strength of the critical components of the floating PV system defined in WP3,
to check the survivability during extreme scenarios (Storm conditions). It is foreseen that the main critical components

                                                                     13
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03

                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

that need to be developed during this project are the connections: They are the weak point of the system with highest
failure rate probability. The task will thus focus on assessing the ultimate strength of the following components:
-Mechanical joint between two breakwater components (to be designed in the project, schematically similar to Figure
1 3 made of steel rope and rubber hinge)
-Connections between breakwater and solar modules (to be designed in the project)
-Connections between solar modules. (polyurethane geometry to be further analysed)
The connections´ prototypes to be tested will be implemented by CLEMENT and SIS. For each of these connections,
main failure modes will be identified (It is foreseen that tension and bending failure will be the two main critical loads,
but this will be confirmed by structural analyses during the project). Results will also be compared to the structural
analyses done in WP5 as validation for the numerical models.
For ultimate strength assessment, the connections will be tested until failure to determine the max load. A minimum of
5 reps will be tested per connections to ensure repeatability of the tests. Since 2 of the mechanical connections of this
project are tailor designed (A combination of materials and joint technology), SINTEF will use standard- and inhouse
test methods, which allow to adapt the setup to specific requests. SINTEF has access to a wide range of servo-hydraulic
machines for testing depending on the load level expected, ranging from a few kN (Dartec) to 1000kN (Schenk-Instron)
as well as temperature chambers and a workshop which can machine any type of specific test setup if required. It is also
planned on using advanced strain measurements technique (DIC) and cameras which will provide detailed information
about the localised deformation of the joints.

Task 6.3: Critical components Fatigue performance (L: SINTEF; P: SIS, CEIT, CLEMENT; Duration: M20-M28):
Task 6.3 aims at investigating the Fatigue life expectancy of the critical connections defined in task 6.2. to this end,
fatigue analyses will be performed on the three mechanical joints. The load amplitude and failure modes for the fatigue
evaluation will be given by the structural analyses developed in WP5 task 5.3 and 5.4, But it is anticipated that tension
for different amplitudes will be the main failure mode and thus the main test configuration (2 different load amplitudes
are planned to be tested to give a rough SN curve for each connection). Fatigue bending will also be envisaged for the
breakwater modules connections (Made of rope) and the connection between break water and solar modules depending
on the connections design chosen in WP3. A minimum of three repetitions should be made per connections assessed and
test configurations (Leading to 18 fatigue tensions tests 12 bending tests). Fatigue tests will be performed using high
frequency magnetic resonance fatigue test machines available such as Amsler 50kN or Instron 100kN depending on the
load amplitude targeted and the design of the mechanical connections which will be developed in WP3.

Task 6.4: Basin system validation (L: MARIN; P: SIS, SINTEF, CEIT; Duration: M13-M26):
The hydrodynamic performance of the combined breakwater and PV modules in both operational and survival sea states
is validated by means of model tests in a wave basin at MARIN. The wave basin is 220 m long, 4 m wide and has a 3.6 m
water depth; the flap-type wave maker is able to generate waves up to 0.7 m wave height. A segment of the breakwater and
PV field including interconnectors will be geometrically reproduced at a model scale of approximately 1:20. The scale
model will be instrumented to measure motions, accelerations, mechanical joints loads, and high-frequency slamming
loads due to breaking wave impacts in operational & survival sea states. Figure 3 3 shows a quasi-2D setup of the model
test in the wave basin with breakwater covering the width of the flume.
The basin test data will be used to tune and validate the hydrodynamic optimization in WP5 and to demonstrate the
TRL5 of the final PV field design. Two test campaigns are foreseen: 1) An initial concept of the combined breakwater-
PV configuration will be tested to measure wave overtopping the breakwater and their impacts onto the PV field as
essential data for the numerical model in T5.2; 2) At final stage, the fully developed FPV field including breakwater and
interconnectors will be reproduced and validated in operational and survival sea states.

Task 6.5: Long-term degradation of the concrete component exposed in harbor (L: ACC; P: CEIT, SINTEF, CLEMENT;
Duration: M20-M36):
The task aims at validating the long-term behaviour of the concrete breakwater solution developed in WP4. Two main
mechanisms are responsible for long term degradation of the concrete in marine environment: biofouling deposition
and corrosion degradation of the rebars. Using sensing technology developed in task 6.1, long term degradation will
be assessed on a small-scale concrete breakwater prototype (1m wide, 0.5m height, 2m length) at the R&D maritime
environment demo site of ACC in Port of Gijón (Spain). During this testing, the monitoring systems will provide valuable
data for the models developed in WP5 as well as insight on the behaviour of the overall technical solutions developed
in WP3 and WP4.

Work package WP7 ­ INTEGRATED SUSTAINABILITY ASSESSMENT

Work Package Number WP7  Lead Beneficiary  7. IFEU

                                                         14
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03
                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

Work Package Name  INTEGRATED SUSTAINABILITY ASSESSMENT

Start Month        1 End Month                           36

Objectives

An integrated sustainability assessment of selected scenarios on the developed floating PV solutions will be performed
taking into account technological, environmental, societal and economic sustainability aspects. This will include the
identification of optimisation potentials as well as conclusions and recommendations on further steps towards a
sustainable implementation of the value chains.

Description

Task 7.1: Sustainability framework definition and mass & energy balances analysis (L: SINTEF; P: IFEU, SIS; Duration:
M1-M24):
This task defines goal and scope questions, general definitions, settings as well as qualitative and quantitative system
and scenario descriptions for T7.2 to T7.6, covering several scenarios to be assessed, based on work in WP2 and in final
design of T3.5, and agreed by all partners in internal workshop in M12.
The quantitative system description will in particular contain complete mass and energy balances of the systems and
reference systems and their operation. SIS will contribute a bill of materials and data on operation with support by WP3
and ACC. To this end, a literature study (supplemented by data from partners and SAB) will be performed, setting the
base-case and reference case.

Task 7.2: Assessment of technological risks and barriers to sustainability (L: SINTEF; P: SIS, IFEU; Duration: M19-
M32):
The technological assessment will evaluate technological indicators of the systems and reference systems defined in
task 7.1. Indicators such as maturity, circularity/suitability for a circular economy, durability and risks for accidents will
be selected and evaluated to capture technological aspects (mainly risks and barriers but also positive drivers) that can
affect sustainability. This will result in a conclusive evaluation of all technological indicators that consists mainly of
semi-quantitative qualitative information.

Task 7.3: Environmental Assessment (L: IFEU; P: SINTEF, SIS, ACC, CLEMENT; Duration: M13-M32):
A particular focus will be on how to reduce GHG emissions while increasing the amount of renewable electricity by
tapping into marine surface area as a resource extending the land surface that can be used for PV, thus contributing to the
objectives of the Mission Healthy oceans. Screening LCAs will follow the guidelines of ISO 14040/14044 on product
LCA and taking into account the ILCD Handbook, EC Recommendation C/2021/9932 on PEF and the DNSH principle.
Potential local/regional environmental impacts of the planned marine installations, which are not covered by LCA, will
additionally be analysed using elements of an environmental impact assessment (EIA).

Task 7.4: Economic assessment (L: SIS; P: SINTEF, ACC, CLEMENT, IFEU; Duration: M19-M32):
The LCoE (considering yield, expected degradation, up-time, O&M, decommissioning) will be calculated for the global
coupled FPV solution resulting from WP3, and will also consider the reduction of the risks impacting on finance and
insurance costs as well as on the production costs owing to reduced GHG anergy footprints (based on a current and
future CO2 tariff predictions). The system will be simulated in different use-case locations (heavily impacting yield,
component and O&M) hence illustrating the economic viability and replicability potential.

Task 7.5: Social assessment (L: IFEU; P: SIS; Duration: M19-M32):
This task will assess social sustainability aspects thus fully integrating SSH into the sustainability assessment framework.
It will be based on the UNEP guidelines (2020) for social LCA and methods from social impact assessment (SIA).
Employment, income, gender, rural development, inclusiveness, labour rights, health and safety, competition about local
use of the marine space.

Task 7.6: Integrated assessment of sustainability (L: IFEU; P: SINTEF, SIS; Duration: M25-M36):
This task will integrate the results from the previous analyses (Tasks 7.2 to 7.5) through a multi-criteria evaluation SW
for the integrated assessment of sustainability covering all sustainability parameters chosen and linking them to UN
sustainable development goals (UN-SDG). This is based on the ILCSA methodology (integrated life cycle sustainability
assessment). Sensitivity analyses will identify parameters crucial for sustainability of the systems to show optimization
potentials. From these analyses, conclusions and recommendations to all relevant stakeholder groups such as developers,
industry and policy makers will be derived.

Work package WP8 ­ DISSEMINATION, COMMUNICATION, EXPLOITATION &
SOCIETAL ENGAGEMENT

Work Package Number WP8  Lead Beneficiary  2. Sunlit Sea

Work Package Name  DISSEMINATION, COMMUNICATION, EXPLOITATION & SOCIETAL
                   ENGAGEMENT

Start Month              1 End Month                                      36

Objectives

The main objective of WP8 is to develop and implement successful strategies for dissemination, communication and
stakeholder engagement. Their ultimate goal is to exploit the project results to maximise impact of the project outcome
and ensure its legacy. The specific objectives of this WP are the following:
-To raise awareness and inform about the project, its results and potential benefits to industry and society
-To disseminate project results in appropriate and impactful channels
-To identify and engage with critical stakeholders from industry, research, certification bodies, media, etc.
-To develop exploitation strategies to ensure that the project will have a long-lasting impact

Description

Task 8.1: Societal Engagement (L: IFEU; Participants: All; Duration: M13-M36):
key stakeholders from diverse societal groups will be identified such as local marine industries, citizens, coastal
associations, etc. with the aim of analysing and defining social needs/requirements by-design (feeding WP2) to ensure
that the FPV design will be compatible with other sea activities (i.e. fishing, fish-farms, maritime navigation, tourism-
leisure or other), also considering the spatial planning aspects. A SWOT workshop with SAB & project partners will
be performed to ensure social aspects consideration in the solution and to support SUREWAVE social awareness &
acceptance.

Task 8.2: Dissemination, Exploitation and Communication Strategy, and Monitoring of its Implementation (L: SIS;
Participants: All; Duration: M1-M36):
This task defines the Dissemination, Exploitation and Communication Master Plan (D8.1) based in communication
measures and targeted audiences are described in section 2.2.
Subtask 8.2.1 Internal Communication Training: within the first 8 months, a course on social media will provide an
insight to the consortium on how to communicate best on relevant issues.
Subtask 8.2.2 Internal Exploitation Workshops: As part of the exploitation strategy, two internal exploitation workshops
will be conducted by all partners to accurately define the definitive Key Exploitable Results (KER).
Subtask 8.2.3. Monitoring: MATOMO (a GDPR version of Google Analytics) will be used for the website, FALCON
platform for social media channels. The results will feed into the second version of DEC Master Plan D8.1 in M18.

Task 8.3: Dissemination Activities (L: SIS; P: All; Duration: M1-M36):
Subtask 8.3.1 Scientific Publications, Scientific Conferences, Fairs and other events. The consortium will produce 10+
articles of peer-reviewed research papers, submitted to open-access scientific journals and technical papers.
Subtask 8.3.2 Technical Brochure. SINTEF & SIS will write a 20+ page technical brochure (D8.3) for industry
stakeholders summarising the results, which will include graphics and photographs and be edited by SIS.
The consortium will contribute, upon invitation by the CINEA, to common information and dissemination activities to
increase the visibility and synergies between Horizon Europe supported actions.

Task 8.4: Exploitation pathways and Business Plan (L: SIS; P: ALL; Duration: M19-M36):
The objective is to work on the basis for the exploitation agreements between technology developers and exploiters,
according to the exploitation strategy and business plan defined in section 2.2. The task will set the ground for decisions
on the business models for technology transfer and the rationale for establishing a fair price. SIS with support and
participation of all the partners will carry out the following steps are considered: 1) Key Exploitable Results (KER)
analysis and IPR analysis; 2) Value innovation analysis and market analysis; 3): Value proposition and value proposition
testing; 4) Development of business models, business plans and exploitation action plans

Task 8.5: Communication activities (L: SIS; Participants: All; Duration: M1-M36):
A variety of promotional and visual content will be developed to share on specialist media publications. The website
will become a meeting place for all stakeholders and will establish links with social media channels and other relevant

                                                                          16
Project: 101083342 -- SUREWAVE -- HORIZON-CL5-2021-D3-03
                                                                                                         Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

projects and initiatives. A community on Twitter and LinkedIn will boost the visibility and impact of the results. A
logo and promotional communication material (1 flyer, rollups) will be developed. 6+ infographics, 3 factsheets, 6+
journalistic articles and 9+ interviews with scientists, engineers and other stakeholders will be developed to approach
the project from different angles. 2 videos (intro and final) will be produced to summarise the results.

In this report, several offshore locations with extreme environmental conditions will be identified and possibility of
corrosion and biofouling will be analysed. The worst-case scenarios for a 20 years return period, in accordance with
DNV RP 0584 will also be defined. Associated to Task 2.1.

breakwater

Description

The report summarizes the results of investigations and determinations of critical components (floating breakwater,
anchoring, connections between breakwater and FPV array). This results in technical requirements that are expressed
as basic design criteria regarding required force transmission, moveability, geometric boundary conditions, material
properties, dynamic behavior, and durability requirements, which serve as a basis for further design of components in
WP3. Associated to tasks 2.2 and 2.3.

1. EXCELLENCE

The European Offshore renewable energy strategy1 highlights the need for more established renewable energy
technologies, and to diversify the technology portfolio to take maximum advantage of the vast potential offered
by EU's seas to become climate neutral by 2050. However, not all offshore renewable technologies are in the same
stage of maturity. Floating Photo-Voltaic (FPV) is in the early R&D stage when it comes to open sea deployment
(Although already deployed in landlocked waters) and more development are needed. Experts have estimated that
the total worldwide capacity of FPV could reach 62 GW by 20302. The limited deployment of offshore FPV is caused
by the intrinsic characteristics of the marine environments (wind loads, wave loads, currents, corrosion, biofouling).
The specific design and structure of the FPV influence its output power generation, durability and investment cost3.
Thus, offshore FPV systems need to be studied in detail (At the design, components, materials and structural levels)
to ensure competitive offshore reliability, lifetime, easy installability and improved O&M, while considering
economic, environmental, and social barriers.
That is why SUREWAVE has seen a clear and huge opportunity in the research and development of a novel and
advanced offshore FPV solution able to unlock the massive offshore FPV deployment in the EU seas. It will take
maximum advantage of previous deep expertise accumulated from the offshore wind field to understand the
floating structures behaviour and how to model those (through SINTEF, MARIN, CEIT) combined with the high
knowledge of development and deployment of FPV in calm waters (SIS) together with floating solutions expertise
of CLEMENT and advanced structural and non-structural materials expertise of ACC. Technical expertise will be
complemented by environmental and socio-economic assessment (IFEU) to overcome non-technical barriers.
SUREWAVE aims to solve the main challenges of offshore FPV by developing a solution adapted to the most critical
sea states, being able to mitigate the harsh conditions for the FPV.
1.1 OBJECTIVES AND AMBITION
1.1.1 OBJECTIVES
The main objective of SUREWAVE is to develop and test an innovative concept of Floating Photo-Voltaic (FPV)
system consisting of an external floating breakwater structure made of new circular materials acting as a
protection against severe wave loads on the FPV structure itself, allowing increased operational availability and
energy output. Such a concept is able to operate in all the European sea-basins including open-sea very harsh
environments with high wind (speed >25 m/s), current (>1.2 m/s) and wave (height >14 m), thus unlocking the
massive deployment of Offshore FPV at European and worldwide level.

1 https://ec.europa.eu/energy/sites/ener/files/offshore_renewable_energy_strategy.pdf
2 https://leadventgrp.com/blog/the-state-of-floating-pv-in-europe-and-asia/
3 Manish Kumar, Humaid Mohammed Niyaz, Rajesh Gupta ­ "Challenges and opportunities towards the development of floating photovoltaic systems" ­ Elsevier 2021)

                                                   101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 2 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

                                                     FIGURE 1-1: CONCEPTUAL APPROACH OF SUREWAVE

The overall project achievement will be accomplished by addressing the following specific objectives:
Technical objectives to address the most relevant TECHNICAL CHALLENGES for the implementation of the
SUREWAVE OFFSHORE FPV SOLUTION:
O1-NOVEL FLOATING PV DESIGN FOR OFFSHORE EXTREME CONDITIONS:

       Research and design on novel breakwater concept with a large wave attenuation effect, as external
           element for the protection and attenuation of the wind, waves, and currents harmful to the internal FPV
           elements whilst ensures that overall structure is safely and non-destructively conducting associated
           forces into the anchorage, not impairing the functionality of the PV-systems.

       Research and optimization of the coupling design between external breakwater and internal FPV,
           including innovative mechanical joints designs to ensure a robust and flexible design and optimal
           structural behaviour.

       Optimizing anchoring and mooring of the full FPV system including it in the whole breakwater design as
           a critical element affecting its structural behaviour owing to extreme offshore conditions.

       Improved energy connection systems through routing and collection of the DC homeruns and through
           the physical interface between floating solar installation and the breakwater.

O2-NEW CIRCULAR CONCRETE MATERIALS DEVELOPMENT:
       Develop and test innovative circular concrete materials solutions for the external protective breakwater,
           enabling reduced environmental impacts with lower CO2 footprint, ensuring structural integrity in severe
           marine environment (very high wind speed >25m/s, current >1.2m/s and waves height >14m) and
           improved material durability through corrosion and biofouling resistance.
       Light weight solution with reduced production & installation costs and easy to cast and transport.

O3-PREDICTIVE COMPUTATIONAL MODELLING & SIMULATION FRAMEWORK DEVELOPMENT COMBINING:
       Improved motion and loading prediction of full FPV system through a high fidelity coupled aerodynamic
           and hydrodynamic modelling tools.
       Structural integrity modelling tool to assess the structural integrity of the most critical parts of the FPV
           solution by means of advanced finite element techniques.
       Artificial Neuronal Network (ANN) based material properties modelling tool for optimization of the
           circular concrete material composition.
       Structural Health Management System (SHMS) to ensure the safe, reliable and durable operation of the
           FPV solution in service and to establish inspection, maintenance and replacement periods by combining
           structural health monitoring, corrosion management and fatigue assessments and thereby reducing

                                                      101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 3 of 39
                                                     Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

CAPEX and OPEX.

O4-ADVANCED TESTING AND VALIDATION FRAMEWORK FOR OFFSHORE FPV technologies with a double purpose:
        To ensure high level of techno-economic feasibility of the solution reducing degradation and failure

            rates while ensuring high availability of system operation offshore in harsh conditions (maximizing energy

            output).
 To achieve high level of fidelity of the simulation framework developed validated by advanced lab testing

    allowing a safe reduction in the over-engineering of devices' specifications.

Non-technical objectives to ensure a feasible, sustainable and highly-replicable DEPLOYMENT OF THE OFFSHORE
FPV SOLUTION INTO THE REAL OFFSHORE ENERGY WORLD. SUREWAVE will consider the following aspects:

O5-SOCIETAL BENEFITS MAXIMIZATION while avoiding potential negative impacts of offshore FPV solution via:
       Social sustainability assessment including social life cycle assessment (sLCA) to identify critical risks early

           on.
       Societal engagement: Identify stakeholders (e.g. in fishing, navigation, tourism) and take their needs e.g.

           on spatial planning into account in designing a compatible solution that receives public acceptance.
       Creating new businesses and job opportunities for on-site shore assembly, transport, installation, O&M.

O6-ENVIRONMENTAL IMPACT MINIMIZATION: SUREWAVE will massively extend the area suitable for large scale
PV installation supporting the EU in powering a decarbonized economy in time for the 1.5°C goal.

       A specific Life Cycle Assessment (LCA) and elements of Environmental Impact Assessment (EIA) will
           ensure that targeted GHG savings are really achieved throughout the supply chain by circularity and that
           no significant harm is done to the marine ecosystem or any other planetary boundary in addition to the
           climate.

       A maximized contribution to the objectives of the Mission Healthy Oceans by-design, aiming to integrate
           with the maritime economy promoting circular, low-carbon, multi-purpose use of marine space.

O7-HIGH ECONOMIC IMPACT to ensure a feasible and scalable uptake of the solution by the market thanks to:
       Reducing Levelized Cost of Energy (LCoE) to around 54 /MWh, making offshore FPV a competitive
           renewable energy (in line with SET Plan targets for offshore wind4, e.g. 35-45 /MWh for fixed, 62-106

           /MWh for floating).
       Ensuring high replicability of the solution, also in less demanding conditions (e.g. Mediterranean Sea).
       Reducing investment risk thanks to smart structural health monitoring and predictive computational

           tools for ensuring structural integrity to reduce failure and to increase installability and maintainability.

O8-HIGH EXPLOITABILITY: In order to achieve best pathways for the commercialization of the global solution a
definitive business model and definitive business plan for exploitation of the solution will be defined. The plan will
include specific Offshore Renewable Energy market complexity, IPR, regulatory framework, market up-take
barriers, and fostering the massive replication into the offshore energy sector.

To this end, SUREWAVE will take the maximum advantage of the existing knowledge on: offshore energies,
structural, aerodynamic & hydrodynamic modelling, monitoring, predictive tools, advanced FPV systems, new
circular materials potential, simulation, testing and lab facilities (see Table 1-1).

still novel for offshore areas which present several disadvantages (exposure to waves, wind loads, salinity, and
