aquatic species, all of which affect the life cycle and efficiency of energy production). There are few studies related
to the FPV systems in offshore/marine environments, however, these studies do not consider the effect of harsh
environment on the performance and operation of the FPV system7 where high levels of fatigue, stress and
corrosion are expected. In order to address these challenges, SUREWAVE proposes a novel and breakthrough
concept for cost-effective and low maintenance Offshore FPV. The project will start at TRL2-3. The FPV solution
will achieve an intermediate TRL3-4 with the design, development and preliminary testing of the materials and
breakwater design and its advanced simulation under the modelling tools developed for the structural integrity
assessment. An iterative process of design, simulation, testing and improvement will be carried out to ensure a
robust and reliable maturity achievement of the solution. Finally, technical validation of some of the critical
components of the system as well a scaled model of the FPV system in emulated offshore conditions will lead the
concept to TRL4-5.

Several technical challenges have been identified to achieve the goal and objectives:

Challenge 1: Dimensioning of the Floating breakwaters including connections and mooring to protect FPV

system against severe load conditions

Currently, floating breakwaters are utilized as protection against short-

period wind waves or ship induced waves in semi sheltered areas such as

lakes, rivers, etc. or near-shore. Even if there are a lot of publications about

several types and shapes of floating breakwaters, the most common type

is the box shape or pontoon type breakwater, usually manufactured with

shell of conventional concrete and with inner core of Expanded Poly- FIGURE 1-3: TYPICAL CONNECTION

Styrene (EPS) with high cost and high CO2 footprint. Flexible connections         BETWEEN BREAKWATER PONTOONS WITH
between the elements allow the arrangement of long floating breakwaters.                   HINGE IN ONE DIRECTION8

These connections are designed to transfer loads in all directions but prevent the transmission of moments in at

least one direction (see Figure 1-38). This is necessary to prevent failure of the components over the entire length.

The efficiency of floating breakwaters is investigated using experimental and numerical techniques. Almost all

publications describe the wave period as the most important criterion for assessing the efficiency and the limiting

design wave period of approx. 4 to 5 seconds. Generally, it is demonstrated that the efficiency decreases with

increasing wave period9.

Nevertheless, the importance of floating breakwaters is growing especially in locations where it is permitted to

fill the sea bottom or where the water depth limits the feasibility of fixed breakwaters. In PIANC's technical

article10 the floating attenuator technology is described as a feasible design solution if the wave damping

efficiency can be maximized.

Furthermore, the Journal of Waterway, Port, Coastal, Ocean Engineering describes the behaviour of elastic

mooring lines on floating breakwaters in swell wave conditions and their relation to each other11

Other publications illustrate the utilization of floating breakwaters to protect embankments in lakes that are at

risk of breaking off, or as a submerged solution to protect against beach erosion.

However, no publication already describes a solution for offshore floating They mainly end up with a description

of the influencing factors and the need for further research.

PROGRESS BEYOND THE STATE OF THE ART:

The research includes the design of connections between single breakwater elements and the design of novel
connection between the breakwater and floating PV module with 3 main functions: maintaining the distance
between breakwater and PV-system, minimize the force transfer from the breakwater to the PV-system, but also
compensate the different dynamic behaviours of breakwater and PV-system by flexibility. There is research
needed to replace EPS materials to more sustainable solutions and to dimension a mooring system that will hold
the breakwater line in place. This will require a multi-objective approach from the modelling and simulation tools
for the structural integrity.

This project aims to show how floating breakwaters can break or attenuate the waves that are harmful to the
floating PV systems. At the same time, the overall structure should safely and non-destructively conduct

8 COPYRIGHT: CLEMENT GERMANY

associated forces into the anchorage also in the case of very long waves, even if they do not impair the
functionality of the floating PV systems.

This is also the main difference from previous studies. Up to now, the focus was set on maximizing the wave-
breaking effect or the effectiveness of the breakwater.

In the project, the behaviour of the floating breakwaters will also be investigated in long waves that cannot be
broken (long wave periods). However, the breakwater pontoons shall be able to transfer the forces into the
anchorage while keeping the floating PV systems securely in place.

The investigation will start with breakwater cross-sections, which are already proven against short-period waves.
The initial part of the research will be focused on a box-shaped cross-section and side wall extension (similar to
catamaran type) to increase draft and attenuating efficiency. However, since side wall extensions are underwater
and the breakwater is completely continuous at the waterline level, this cross-section is globally not a catamaran.

As a next step, different geometrical and weight ratios of each module will be researched and simulated at the
aero and hydrodynamic levels to define the best structural integrity solution. As a result of the investigations and
under consideration of the new circular materials, size, weight, and shape of the breakwater pontoons might
differ from the initial box-shaped cross-section.

Challenge 2: Development of new circular concrete materials for structural offshore component ­ breakwater
shell
The shell of the breakwater is made of conventional concrete, but SUREWAVE aims to design advanced concrete
formulation to optimise the weight to performance ratio: Two formulations are investigated in this project: The
UHPFRC (Ultra-High Performance Fibre-Reinforced Concrete) and/or LWAC (Light Weight Aggregate Concrete):
Ultra-High-Performance Fibre-Reinforced Concrete (UHPFRC): 1) optimized packing density of the matrix, with
no coarse aggregates; 2) ductility enhancement by introduction of fibers to obtain strain-hardening behaviour in
tension; 3) compressive strength in the range of 120 or more MPa (very much higher than the conventional
reinforce concrete ­ 35-60 MPa); 4) ability to self-heal and seal cracks during their lifetime thanks to its own
autogenous healing capacity and to the addition of crystalline waterproofing admixtures; and 5) high durability
is achieved due to of high compactness and low w/b, therefore, the total amount of pores in the microstructure
is very low (from 3 to 5 %) and they are non-interconnected scale. This feature gives them quite exceptional
performance in terms of durability: water porosity, air permeability, freeze-thaw cycles resistance and chloride-
ion diffusion factors are greatly reduced compared to ordinary or HPC. In addition, the fibers contribute to the
ability of delaying crack propagation in the hardened concrete12. UHPFRC is increasingly being used in structural
elements subjected to dynamic loads, for instance in offshore applications or bridge constructions and recent
H2020 projects (e.g. LORCENIS or ReSHEALience) are researching on UHPRFCs for coastal and offshore energy
applications. However, UHPFRC has some drawbacks that will be addressed and overcome in SUREWAVE Project:
1) Higher shrinkage than conventional concrete, which can be the cause of micro- and macrocracking, and could
limit the range of applications; 2) Higher CO2 footprint due to the high amount of cement required; 3) Higher
cost due to the high amount of cement and steel fibers. Depending on the cement and fibre content, material
costs for the concrete per m3 is expected to be -5 to 10 times higher than standard concrete.
Lightweight Aggregate Concrete (LWAC) is another interesting alternative for the shell: the expanded clay
aggregates are usually used, obtaining a concrete with the same strength than the conventional concrete (40
MPa) but lower density (1900 kg/m3 instead of 2400 kg/m3). The main drawback of expanded clay is its high CO2
footprint due to the thermal treatment used in the production process of these aggregates.
PROGRESS BEYOND THE STATE OF THE ART:

SUREWAVE Project aims to reduce the high amount of cement necessary in UHPFRC (from 850 kg cement/m3 of
concrete to 350 kg/m3) by substituting it with a combination of ground granulated blast furnace slag (GGBFS)

12 Hassanpour M, Shafigh P, Mahmud HB (2012) Lightweight aggregate concrete fiber reinforcement--a review. Constr Build Mater 37:452­461

                                                   101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 6 of 39
                                                                                                            Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

and fly ash (class F), both industrial waste materials. In both cases, also silica fume is used (a co-product from the
production of silicon or ferrosilicon metal). Studies show that larger amounts of cement (up to 60 %) can be
replaced in UHPFRC by fly ash and GGBFS without any loss of mechanical performance13. Several aspects will be
improved at once thanks to this substitution: Sustainability of the concrete (Cement is the main contributor to
the CO2 footprint), its durability both for the pozzolanic effect and for the shrinkage reduction. Furthermore, the
overall cost will be significantly lower, as cement is the most expensive component. Also, the shell will be
thinner by design thanks to the higher strength. Apart from the binder optimization, the fine aggregates can be
replaced partially by circular materials, such as Construction and demolition waste and recycled glass aggregates.
An additional technical challenge is the early strength development, which is less pronounced when higher
replacement levels of fly ash and GGBFS are present in the material.

On the other hand, with respect to the LWAC, SUREWAVE Project 1) will add recycled glass aggregates with the
advantage of decreasing the CO2 footprint by using lightweight aggregates coming from waste and 2) will reduce
the amount of cement used by adding mineral admixtures up to a 80% (mainly GGBFS and microsilica, but also
FA) with the advantages already commented for the UHPFRC.

Challenge 3: Development of new Circular concrete materials for non-structural offshore component ­

Breakwater inner core

Current material used in the core of the breakwater is expanded polystyrene (EPS), which is made from styrene
with an expansion gas. It has a very low density (from 10 to 60 kg/m3). However, this material has important

drawbacks: 1) it can dissolve in water and be harmful to the marine life; 2) high CO2 footprint (639 CO2eq/m3)14

due to customized cut at the industrial chemical plant and transported to the breakwater production facility.

PROGRESS BEYOND THE STATE OF THE ART:

The solution offered consists in a mineral based concrete material: Cellular Lightweight Concrete (CLC). The

material is lightweight with densities between 200 and 300 kg/m3, and has the following advantages with respect

to the EPS: 1) stable in water, not dangerous to the environment; 2) conventional CLC has a lower CO2 footprint
than EPS (302 CO2 eq/m3) 15. In SUREWAVE it will be reduced even more thanks to the use of GGFS (Ground-

granulated blast-furnace slag) and fly ash; besides, due to its inorganic nature, it is easy to recycle; and finally 3)

behaving as a fluid, it can be casted and filled the directly at the breakwater production facility.

Challenge 4: Development of coupled - aerodynamic modelling and optimization tool

The application of Coupled hydro- and aero-dynamic models, to quantify motions and slamming loads in severe
sea states16, has been used in offshore wind turbines and oil platforms17 and is in the present project extended

to a "flat" PV system subjected to simultaneous wind and wave loading. This means that previously unexplored

physical processes become of major concern (e.g., the lifting force (wind getting underneath the PV modules),

multi-body interactions, breakwater over-topping, and slamming forces). In addition, regarding the automatic

optimization of offshore systems, for instance through genetic algorithms, these kinds of tools are becoming

increasingly popular in the design of ships and offshore structures. By coupling 3D CAD SW with hydrodynamic

calculation tools and optimization algorithms (e.g., DAKOTA), an offshore structure can be efficiently optimized

area and the interconnection loads between breakwater pontoons, thus bringing an innovative approach to the

Challenge 5: Development of novel Material properties prediction model for Concrete shell

It is complex to predict all the structural features of a concrete material in a single microstructural model because

of the wide range of feature scales that can affect the observed mechanical behaviour. However, different ways

can be employed to tackle this problem:

Multiscale Modelling (MM) aims to predict homogenized properties at macrolevels using micromechanics-based

models with focus on the following three aspects: composition and structure; mechanical performance; and
properties relating to the durability of concrete21. Computations performed at one level provide input properties

to be used in simulations of performance at the next higher level22 . However, the computational costs are very

high and increase with the number of model scale to be included.

Simple approaches have also been developed which mainly rely on empirical rules and testing to model Normal
Weight Concrete, and Light Weight Aggregates Concretes (LWAC)23. These methods are easy to formulate and

are computationally effective, however they are specific to a certain type of concrete, making the methodology

difficult to extend and generalize.

Artificial Neural Network (ANN) is a novel alternative offering promising approach to predict the concrete

behaviour by using ANN regression methods instead of linear regression models. ANN approach has been used

for example to predict: 1) The compressive strength of silica fume concrete24, 2) The Water permeability,

compressive strength, and the porosity of the concrete with volcanic scoria (VS); 3) to find optimum content of

supplementary cementing materials (SCMs) such as limestone filler (LF), blended with Portland cement25. The

different studies reveal that the ANNs modelling approach is a highly efficient tool for improving concrete

mechanical strength prediction.

PROGRESS BEYOND THE STATE OF THE ART:

The regression technique using ANN seems promising but has not been extensively used and validated for LWA

such as recycled aggregates and substitutes to cement (granulated blast furnace slag (GBFS) or fly ash) developed

Challenge-6: Development and validation of Structural Health Management to optimize FPV system

The traditional approach to deal with structural integrity and service life of components has been the use of S-N
(Stress-Failure) curves to determine how long a component will last in service26. The two main disadvantages of

this method are the costly expenses of continuously replacing components that may still have remaining life

and the premature failure of these components. Structural Health Monitoring can provide valuable data

(current and historical) about the behaviour and evolution of the structure in service, which can be converted for
damage identification and preventive maintenance through statistical techniques27. However, this approach

does not describe the physics of the structure nor perform structural analysis to assess the acceptability of flaws,

an essential information for predictive maintenance and design optimization. Finally, fractures mechanics take

advantage of non-destructive testing technologies (crack monitoring) to assess the remaining life of components

through numerical and advanced finite element techniques. However, these calculations are computationally

expensive and can only be performed by users with advanced knowledge and skills in modelling.

PROGRESS BEYOND THE STATE OF THE ART:

A Structural Health Management System (SHMS) that combines structural monitoring with numerical modelling

will be developed and implemented to perform accurate structural integrity diagnoses, to calculate the remaining

fatigue life of components and to establish maintenance, inspection and replacement periods for these

components. To assess the structural assessment quickly enough, without the need of performing expensive

finite element-based procedures, fatigue crack propagation algorithms with approximate techniques for

calculating Stress Intensity Factors (SIFs) in simplified planar and three-dimensional geometries will be

implemented. Crack propagation laws are generically known as the Paris-Erdogan laws, with their modifications

to take into account the effects of the load ratio and crack closure due to plasticity, environmental effects, etc.

Besides, for calculating the SIFs the solutions available in literature as well as approximate solutions generated

from finite elements will be used. Finally, the SHMS will be deployed in the form of easy-to-use App, allowing

non-expert users to be guided performing the desired calculation

1.2 METHODOLOGY

1.2.1 CONCEPTS & MODELS UNDERPINNING THE PROJECT

As introduced before, SUREWAVE aims to research, develop, and test an advanced offshore FPV solution taking
advantage of the existing expertise in offshore energy structures (ACC, CLEMENT), FPV landlocked waters (SIS), and
huge potential of material and structural predictive modelling (SINTEF, CEIT), able to unlock the deployment of FPV
in any kind of offshore scenario.

As introduced before, SUREWAVE aims to research, develop, and test an advanced offshore FPV solution taking
advantage of the existing expertise in offshore energy structures (ACC, CLEMENT), FPV landlocked waters (SIS), and
huge potential of material and structural predictive modelling (SINTEF, CEIT), able to unlock the deployment of FPV
in any kind of offshore scenario.

SUREWAVE proposes to solve the main challenges of offshore FPV deployment in open sea by developing a solution
that can smoothly be adapted to the most critical ocean conditions scenario, being able firstly to attenuate the
harsh conditions for the FPV and secondly to resist to the quick variability of diverse mechanical (aerodynamic &
hydrodynamic) and degrading conditions of marine environment. To have a clear understanding of such technical
challenges, first it is necessary to have an overview of the main technical concepts of the proposed solution (see

26 A. Wholer, Uber die Festigkeitsversuche mit Eisen und Stahl, Zeitschrift fur Bauwesen, vol. 20, pp. 73­106, 1870
27 Thibbotuwa, U.C.; Cortés, A.; Irizar, A. Ultrasound-Based Smart Corrosion Monitoring System for Offshore Wind Turbines. Appl. Sci. 2022, 12, 808.

                                                      101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 9 of 39
                                                                                                               Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

Figure 1-1). SUREWAVE concept is based on the development on an innovative external floating breakwater
structure made of new circular material solutions to attenuate and minimize the adverse effects of open-sea
harsh conditions for the FPV structure present inside. It is very inspired from harbour construction where the ships
are protected from sever sea states by a protective barrier. This unique design philosophy will allow FPV to increase
operational window and energy output while ensuring the structural integrity of the complete system in open sea.
Another advantage is that it will also minimise the future effort of adaptation of the internal FPV components and
intermodular connection systems, that won't need to be overengineered for increased survivability, thus
minimising future development costs of such systems.

                                                        FIGURE 1-4: SUREWAVE CONCEPT WITH PILLARS

The concept underpinning SUREWAVE is laying on three pillars (Figure 1-4):
        Pillar-1: R&D of the innovative breakwater concept for the Offshore FPV to be operated in harsh
            conditions.

 Pillar-2: Development of sustainable new circular materials with improved techno-economic-environmental
    performance

 Pillar-3: Intelligent predictive modelling tools to ensure an optimal structural integrity, reliability, durability,
    and improved O&M.

PILLAR-1: R&D of the innovative breakwater concept for the Offshore FPV to be operated in harsh conditions.
The focus of first main pillar of SUREWAVE is to minimize the effect of the offshore extreme conditions of very high
wind (speed >25 m/s), current (>1.2 m/s) and wave (height >14 m) on the operability of the PV system. SUREWAVE
strategy is to enclose the fragile PV elements into a protective barrier, instead of over engineering the PV elements
itself. The external structural element's (protective barrier) main function is to protect the FPV modules by
efficiently minimizing and reducing the wave effect into the protected area. This will maximise the operational
window of the energy system and limited loads on the PV system will increase the lifespan of the system and
decrease the maintenance operations.
Moreover, limited loads on the PV system will increase the lifespan of the system and decrease the maintenance
operations. SUREWAVE's solutions for building this external protective barrier is to rely on the breakwater
technology (CLEMENT's main field of expertise) whose survivability is optimised for severe loads in terms of
dimensions, geometry, and materials. This solution has never been implemented in offshore scenario before. If the
idea of the concept is not to modify the modular PV modules itself which will be protected from the most severe
mechanical loads, improvements of the individual critical components of the internal FPV structure will be needed
in SUREWAVE: There is more specifically innovations needed in the design of mechanical joints between the
modules and the coupling between the breakwater line and the FPV modules for such a system to become
successful.
The SUREWAVE decision on basing the concept on the development of a protective novel offshore breakwater is
clearly justified considering the analysis of the offshore extreme conditions. It is estimated that the protection
from the breakwater will be maximised for wave periods distributed around 4 to 5s, which are the most detrimental
wave periods for the installation. Especially the high-frequency wave energy content is critical to the structural

                                                   101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 10 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

integrity of the PV, as it may induce (i) high slamming loads on the PV through wave breaking and (ii) large
overturning motions of the PV, leading to large inter-connector loads as well as the risk of large wind lift loads when
the bottom of the PV panels emerges from the waterline.

Therefore, the floating breakwater will be designed to break or to attenuate the high-frequency wave energy to an
acceptable limit. It is however not the intention of this project to damp the longer waves period (swell waves).
SUREWAVE will rely on the high flexibility of the FPV system which will allow the structure to follow the largest
wave period. Moreover, the probability of occurrence of such wind and wave events is expected to be very low,
maybe once a year or even less frequently.

The breakwater is conceived to be shaped as a modular perimetral system

made of multiple modules of individual breakwaters.

m (wide), 2.5 m (height), 20 meters (length). Different geometrical

and weight ratios of each module will be researched and simulated

at aero and hydrodynamic level to define the best structural

shaped cross section and side wall extension (similar to catamaran

type) to increase draft and attenuating efficiency.

PILLAR-2: Development of sustainable new circular materials with improved techno-economic impact and reduced
environmental footprint

Concerning the breakwater properties, it is conceived to be a

high performance sustainable product, with low environmental

footprint, and combining corrosion and biofouling resistance.

Its high mechanical properties should make it highly-efficient at

damping critical aero-hydro-dynamic loads while ensuring very

high lifespan. In addition, it should be easy to cast, easy to

produce and transport and cost-efficient. To combine all these

characteristics into a novel structural element not developed for

such conditions before, SUREWAVE will propose new circular

concrete material solutions. The breakwater can be mainly FIGURE 1-7: ILLUSTARTION OF THE INNER CORE AND OUTER
decomposed in two parts (Figure 1-7)28: an external structural SHELL STRUCTURE OF A BREAK WATER WITH DIFFERENT

shell and an internal non-structural inner core. It is the         MATERIAL SOLUTIONS INVESTIGATED IN SUREWAVE

combination of these two parts that gives a very strong and lightweight component (A technology similar to

sandwich materials). Within SUREWAVE, it is aimed at optimising both parts of the breakwater.

28 Original illustration (without SUREWAVE comments and materials) of a schematic example of typical pontoon breakwater from MARINETEK

                                                     101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 11 of 39
                                                                                                               Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

On one hand, several structural circular material solutions will be researched for the structural shell, mainly
analysing Ultra High Performance Concrete (UHPC) and Light weight aggregates concrete (LWAC). The aim is to use
recycled aggregates and to replace part of the cement with granulated blast furnace slag (GBFS) or fly ash, as well
as to boost corrosion and biofouling resistance. In addition, to boost their durability, these materials will have the
ability to self-heal and seal cracks during their lifetime thanks to its own autogenous healing capacity and to the
addition of crystalline waterproofing admixtures.

On the other hand, non-structural cellular lightweight cement-based materials will be researched for the inner
core with special focus on developing low CO2 and waterproof lightweight inorganic formulations including waste
materials such as fly ash and slags.

PILLAR-3: Development of the intelligent predictive computational modelling tools (serving both for the design
phase and for the structural health management for optimal O&M), to ensure an optimal structural integrity,
reliability and durability of the Offshore FPV.

The last pillar of SUREWAVE is the structural integrity as the main driver of the future offshore FPV. The offshore
structures integrity is critical because of high probability of component failure with higher maintenance costs. This
O&M issue is the main cost driver of the offshore energies which makes the critical difference between offshore
FPV and landlocked FPV. That is why, the main focus will be to highly secure an optimal structural behaviour and
structural integrity of the FPV system as a whole.

The breakwater system by itself does not guarantee that the whole structural health is controlled and behaving
properly specially in some sea sates where the damping effects of the breakwater are limited. In that case, both
the breakwater integrity and the inner connections might be subjected to severe loads. In addition, the corrosion
effects on the whole system are one of the main causes of offshore long term structural failure. Over time both
critical phenomena (corrosion and aero-hydrodynamic extreme conditions) will affect the offshore PV.

To this end, SUREWAVE aims to develop and establish a predictive computational modelling and simulation
framework to design, predict and ensure structural integrity and increased lifespan of the offshore FPV system as
a whole, which includes several modelling modules and computational steps:

        Advanced material properties modelling tool for the circular concrete materials, including regression
            ANN empirical-based model to predict the mechanical properties for different types of aggregates. It will
            predict compressive strength of the concrete for given density levels of LWA, strength to density ratios
            and porosities.

        Novel aero-hydro-dynamic simulation tool coupling aerodynamic and hydrodynamic effects as an
            integral effects scenario. Besides, SUREWAVE expects to develop an optimization toolkit to allow
            combined simulations including linearized wave-induced response of FPV with external breakwater, and
            able to optimize the configuration in terms of motions and interconnection loads.

        Structural integrity modelling tool to assess the structural integrity of the most critical parts of the PFV
            solution following required specifications and standards by means of advanced finite element techniques.

        Fatigue crack propagation simulation tool to predict the fatigue life of the critical parts of the structure
            and to evaluate their reliability and durability under predicted working conditions.

 Structural Health Management System (SHMS) to ensure the safe, reliable and durable operation of the FPV
    solution in service and to establish inspection, maintenance and replacement periods by combining structural
    health monitoring, corrosion management and fatigue assessments.

Advanced aero-hydro-dynamic simulation tools will be utilized
to analyse wave-wind-current effects on the breakwater to find

consisting of 10 breakwaters. Optimization techniques will be
used to design the breakwaters to minimise the wave height
behind the breakwaters in the FPV area and the mechanical
loads exchanged between the breakwaters. All investigations of
the geometry also take into account the possibilities of
anchoring in terms of making them less complex as possible.

The result of this research will be that innovative Structural Health Management System (SHMS) in the form of
easy-to-use-app (enabling its use by non-experts) for in-service structural integrity management. The SHMS
platform will serve as the toolkit for the design, modelling and simulation and then for the fine-tune design of
the SUREWAVE offshore FPV system.

1.2.2 THE OVERALL METHODOLOGY

The methodology followed in the project can be split into three main phases, each connected to a WP structure
and tasks (see Figure 3-1):

PHASE 1) Offshore FPV ­ GLOBAL FRAMEWORK DEFINITION

This phase will be carried out through the implementation of the WP2 (General framework definition) and also with
task 8.1 (Societal engagement) to avoid social barriers. It aims at establishing a basis, general framework,
specifications and base case concepts of the global FPV solution to give a common and coherent starting point for
the main technical developments to be addressed in phase 2 "technology development". In addition, the
requirements for installation, operation, maintenance, and decommissioning will be also considered in this phase.

The First step in establishing use-case scenarios is the selection of a few actual locations, which are suitable for the
deployment of FPV system, and from which metocean data will be collected and forecasts models estimated. The
locations will have an impact on the establishment of the worst-case scenarios and thus on the main requirements
potentially affecting the structural integrity of the solution. It will also give indication to the estimation of the
potential power production as well as potential preliminary use-case transport, installation, O&M framework
(available facilities, equipment, costs, timing, etc.). These locations will be carefully selected to represent a variety
of European sea conditions and analysed to define a set of characteristic use cases scenario, global requirements
and critical aspects to be taken into account.

The site selection will enable to accurately establish both the technical specifications to solve the identified
technical challenges (Challenge:1-5) and the basis of the non-technical requirements to solve the potential
barriers at social, economic, and environmental level as well as to successfully addressing other potential barriers
as regulatory or standardisation among others, to ensure future feasible scale-up, deployment and replication.

Furthermore, a preliminary study of the critical components will be carried out in Phase 1 mainly focused on: 1)
Breakwater (as external protective-damping structural element); 2) Anchoring/mooring; 3) Mechanical
joints/connexion elements; 4) Energy connection systems; as well as an analysis focusing on the integration
requirements among the different key elements involving transversal disciplines (electric system, transport,

                            101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 13 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

installation, O&M, decommissioning) but also with specific emphasis on specifying the needs on the mechanical
joining of the breakwater and FPV array as a critical aspect potentially affecting both the structural system
behaviour and also the cost of the solution. The results of this stage will be the reference point for the other two
phases at development and validation level, but also for the analysis of exploitation plan and business strategy for
the SUREWAVE deployment. Offshore FPV ­ TECHNOLOGIES DEVELOPMENT

PHASE 2) Offshore FPV ­ TECHNOLOGIES DEVELOPMENT

From the scenario and requirements defined in phase 1, the novel FPV design will

be implemented through the WP3 of the work plan, using numerical tools

developed in WP5 and material development in WP4 to optimise the full system.

The system consists of a breakwater line, protecting a modular floating PV array,  FIGURE 1-10: MODULES

joined together by special connections, and fixed in position with a mooring ASSEMBLED IN COMPLETE STRINGS

system. It is thus critical to the success of the project to define a multi-scale methodology which not only considers

each individual components behaviour but also the interaction between the different components of the system

to optimise the solution. To this end a set of predictive computational modelling and simulation tools integrating

material properties modelling, hydro-aerodynamic simulations, and structural integrity management of the FPV

solution will be developed in WP5 as support to WP3.

SUREWAVE approach to increase the durability of the FPV system and its integrity relies on a protective line of
breakwaters around FPV modules from Sunlit Sea but could potentially be applied to other floating PV concepts.
Thus, the main focus of the project is not the full optimisation of the Sunlit Sea solar PV module, although it was
selected for good reasons: The SIS concept is a very flat solar solution (minimizing wind drag) based on individual
modules (Made from pressed, rolled marine grade aluminium, see Figure 1-10) assembled into prefabricated
complete strings. Its light, aluminium shell design makes it very resistant to external loads and to corrosion. Its
modular conception makes it easy to be stacked into a common shipping container/ barge deck, enabling rapid
deployment, and covering large area on the water. One critical point of every modular design, including SIS
solutions are the flexible connections between the modules (Challenge 1) which will be specifically improved in
SUREWAVE by means of structural analyses (WP3, WP5) and validation in WP6.

The design and dimensioning of the external breakwater including anchoring and mooring systems is key to the
concept (Pillar-1) and will thus be carried out to provide optimal wave damping frequency range to attenuate the
locally generated wind waves (WP3). The global shape of the breakwater perimeter (Circular, square or others as
described in Pillar-1 and shown in Figure 1-6), but also its geometry (optimal shape, see Figure 1-5), weight and
dimensions are design parameters to be optimised (Challenge 1). The Beaufort scale of wind speeds will be followed
with variable dimensions at real possible wave spectra in offshore areas, aiming to tackle: the wave breaking or
attenuating effect (proportion of reflection and transmission), the dynamic behaviour, the internal load carrying
capacity and force characteristics and transmission to couplings.

The anchoring and mooring systems will be dimensioned according to the definitive shape of the individual
breakwater modules and of the global perimetral shape of breakwater. The anchoring solution chosen will influence
the distribution of the loads between the breakwater, mooring and modules. Particular attention will be paid to
excessive forces on the mooring attachment points and snapping-forces on the mooring attachment points/lines
and anchors in case of rigid motions of the FPV array due to changing wind direction. This research will be supported
by the advanced coupled aero-hydrodynamic analysis and design optimization tool (Challenge 4) developed in
WP5 (Pillar-3) which will give accurate estimations on the forces distribution and local motions of the system. These
analyses consist of three steps: 1) aerodynamic assessment of the breakwater in relevant inflow conditions (wind
speed, direction, turbulence level) of the complete FPV system; 2) hydrodynamic assessment to characterize the
wave-related hydrodynamic loads distinguishing between severe (survival scenario) and mild-moderate sea states
(operational scenario). Their probability of occurrence and the impact on the breakwater, internal FPV modules
and mooring lines will be assessed; 3) Coupled aero-hydrodynamic analysis and design optimization under DAKOTA
optimization toolkit to accurately determine the required gap between the breakwater and the PV panel modules
in worst-case scenarios.

101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 14 of 39
                                                                  Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

The breakwater itself will endure severe loads as a protective barrier (Wave slamming and pressure load from the

waves are two main load scenarios). Particular attention will thus be placed on optimising its durability (Structural

integrity and resistance to long term degradation in marine environment). This will be obtained through the

development of innovative new circular concrete solutions constituting the core and the shell of the breakwater

structure (Pillar-2). The new circular material solutions will be developed in WP4, based on preliminary

specifications on the breakwater and global floating PV design in WP3 and on the pressures and forces estimated

by the models in WP5. The selection of the optimal solution at the end of the project

will be a combination of alternative options of structural shell solutions with the

inner core solutions. Concerning the new circular material solutions for the structural

shell of the breakwater (Challenge-2), the strategy followed in SUREWAVE relies on

analysing a set of new circular mixes designs based on low CO2 structural concrete

formulations of two different approaches: Ultra High-Performance Concrete (see

example of composition in Figure 1-1129) and Light weight aggregates concrete. This

research will be supported by the implementation of an efficient computational

modelling tool for concrete material properties in WP5, using promising ANN              FIGURE 1-11: TYPICAL

Model Order Reduction techniques to overcome (Challenge-5) and enabling to COMPOSITION OF K-ULTRA-HIGH

investigate a wider variety of compositions than the standard experimental-only PERFORMANCE CONCRETE (K-UHPC)

based approach. For the inner core of the breakwater, a non-structural cellular lightweight cement-based material

will be investigated as replacement to the non-sustainable EPS material (Challenge 3). Special focus will be put on

developing low CO2 and waterproof lightweight inorganic formulations, stable in marine environment, including

in the formulation waste materials such as fly ash and slags and waterproofing agents. Although individual analysis

and testing of their mechanical, physical and durability properties will be done, special emphasis will be put on the

durability assessment of the interface between the shell and the inner core, which will be critical to assure later

mechanical integrity of the breakwater component to be developed. For the preliminary validation of the new

material solution SUREWAVE will cast a small prototype of the breakwater to be tested at lab scale TRL4, with a

reduced scale of 1 m (wide), 0.5 m (heigh), 2 m (length). It will be further validated at harsh environmental

conditions in the "PHASE 3" (see below) achieving a TRL5 in WP6.

A last critical point to be addressed in the technical development phase is the connections between the different
components of the FPV system (Pillar-1), in particular the connections point between the PV modules and
breakwater line (Challenge 1) to ensure safe, reliable, and durable operation. SUREWAVE strategy to solve that
issue is to perform Structural integrity assessments (Pillar-3) of the most critical parts of the structure using a
combination of analytical, numerical, and advanced finite element techniques. In particular, strength and fatigue
assessments of these components will be performed through advanced Fracture Mechanics techniques and FEA
taking into account the predicted working conditions (corrosion, environmental effects, nodal
forces/displacements) and required specifications and standards.

Besides, SUREWAVE will develop a user-friendly Structural Health Management System (SHMS) by combining
numerical modelling and structural monitoring (Challenge 6). For this purpose, fatigue crack propagation algorithm
for simplified 2D and 3D crack fronts will be developed and integrated into FEA simulation. A comparative validation
of the computational tools will be done to calibrate and validate the developed modelling and simulation tools
using the data produced by targeted validation campaigns (WP4 for the breakwater circular material properties
and WP6 for the whole system).

PHASE 3) Offshore FPV ­ TESTING AND VALIDATION

The third phase of SUREWAVE aims to mature the offshore FPV technology up to TRL5 using a double assessment
approach: Technical assessment and integral sustainability assessment:

The technical testing and validation of the SUREWAVE offshore FPV solution will be assessed through the
implementation of the WP6 of the work plan (Pillar-1 and 2). The main WP objective is to make the technical
validation of the global Floating PV solution developed in WP3, and the model tools developed in WP4 and WP5.
This Validation will include lab testing (through advanced hydrodynamic test bench facilities of MARIN and
structural laboratory at SINTEF) and sea-exposed validation of the concrete element for long term degradation and
biofouling assessment to mature the solution to a TRL5, considering harsh conditions. In addition, this technical
assessment aims to achieve and validate a robust and reliable simulation framework that will allow to optimize

29 Park, J.-S.; Kim, Y.J.; Cho, J.-R.; Jeon, S.-J. Early-Age Strength of Ultra-High Performance Concrete in Various Curing Conditions. Materials 2015, 8, 5537-5553

                                                     101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 15 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

the FPV solution thus having a better understanding of the device's real-life performance allowing a safe reduction
in the over-engineering of devices' specifications, lowering the cost at the time than reducing investment risks.

SUREWAVE will define and deploy an advanced sensing and monitoring solution to allow adequate
implementation of the basin tests and for the long-term validation. The monitoring system will enable to gather
data during the testing phase, which will be used to validate the models and SHMS developed in WP5. The
monitoring system will be capable of detecting failures on critical points of the structure so that the designer can
optimize the properties of the material and/or the geometry of the structure for future improved designs.

The critical mechanical connections will be assessed in SUREWAVE to ensure that the ultimate strength
performance of the identified critical mechanical joints (connections between two breakwater components,
connections between breakwater and solar modules, connections between solar modules) have sufficient
structural capacity. Furthermore, fatigue performance testing will be carried out to validate the fatigue life
expectancy considering the load amplitude values obtained from structural analysis tools developed in WP5.

The hydrodynamic performance of the combined external breakwater and internal FPV modules in both
operational and survival sea states (High frequency slamming loads and breaking waves) will be validated by means
of model tests in a wave basin at MARIN advanced facilities. The detailed description and dimensions along with
the test campaigns are given in WP6.

Finally, to complete the technical testing and validation phase, SUREWAVE will validate the long-term behaviour
of the concrete breakwater solution (Pillar-2) under normal operating condition (ACC test site) testing of
biofouling and corrosion of the rebars of the concrete breakwater prototype built within WP4 on which the
advanced sensing and structural health monitoring will be deployed to provide valuable data for the models and
methodologies developed in WP5 as well as insight on the behaviour of the technical solutions developed in WP3
and WP4.

In parallel an accurate integral sustainability assessment will be performed, through WP7, to solve the potential
non-technical barriers and facilitating a high-replicable deployment of the solution into the real offshore energy
world. This analysis is composed of 4 main approaches: Environmental, Economic, Social and integrated approach.

The first step will be the definition and implementation of the mass and energy balance considering materials used,
energy consumed for the whole life-cycle (production of components, pre-assemblies, transport, installation, O&M
and decommissioning) both for the SUREWAVE FPV solution as well as for a baseline system.

Then once available the mass-energy balances, to ensure the minimization of the environmental impact, a Life-
Cycle Assessment (LCA) of the developed solution assuming the potential full-scale future deployment into the
marine environment will be done, so that the ultimate objectives of the project for increased clean energy
generation, GHG emissions reduction, and minimization of product footprint in-land (production) and offshore
(installation, O&M, decommissioning) are duly accomplished.

Secondly, to achieve a high economic impact to ensure a feasible adaptation of the solution by the market, an
accurate methodology will be defined and implemented for calculating the LCoE of the SUREWAVE solution,
conceived to ensure replicability in different sea basins searching for economic feasibility not only the application
in harsh environments but also analysing routes of SUREWAVE easy adaptations for high replicability potential on
less demanding conditions (e.g. Mediterranean sea).

Then an integral social Life Cycle Assessment (sLCA) will be carried out to analyse social impacts of the researched
activities on a variety of stakeholders including competition and synergies with other sea activities (i.e. fishing, fish-
farms, maritime navigation, tourism-leisure) to identify critical risks early on ensure social acceptance.

Once the environmental, economic and social aspects have been duly analysed and assessed, a final step of
integrated sustainability assessment will be performed by integrating these 3 aspects through the use of a multi-
criteria evaluation SW tool for the integrated assessment of sustainability covering all sustainability parameters
chosen and linking them to UN sustainable development goals (UN-SDG).

Finally, through WP8 SUREWAVE will maximize impact, including specific communication and dissemination
activities targeting both scientific and industry stakeholders, as the project is expected to have a strong impact in
both areas. A robust exploitation plan will also be elaborated to ensure and maximize the successful uptake of the
results. It will include the development of a business plan for the replication of the results thanks to the Structural
Health Management System integrating a complete simulation framework useful as a design and dimensioning tool
for any offshore FPV design, lowering the design costs and de-risking the design and implementation of the solution.

1.2.4 INTER-DISCIPLINARY APPROACH
 The project covers an interdisciplinary approach combining experts in ocean energy, materials, analytic modelling

 tools, involving the main disciplines for the development of an FPV solution optimal for offshore conditions:
        Wind and aerodynamic analysis and modelling of the resulting floating PV solution
        Hydrodynamic analysis, modelling and assessment, including advanced and accurate knowledge and SW
            tools for CFD (Computational Fluid Dynamics) Development

 Research on new circular concrete materials (Ultra High-Performance Concrete - UHPC, Light Weight
    aggregates concrete and lightweight cellular concrete) with low carbon footprint using recycled light weight

    aggregates, replacing cement with granulated blast furnace slag, fly ash...
 Material properties modelling for new circular materials
 Structural integrity modelling and predictive AI/ML management computational expertise as well as advanced

    structural health monitoring systems.
 Novel floating structures optimal for damping and minimizing offshore demanding conditions at the time that

    optimally integrating floating PV modules structures
        Offshore knowledge on regulation, installation, operation, maintenance, demanding conditions to
            ensure increased lifespan at component and global structural level
        Advanced simulation and testing facilities HW, SW and expertise for the validation of the global floating
            offshore PV solution up to TRL5, accurately simulating open sea harsh conditions.
        Specific expertise in social, economic and environmental assessment of offshore energy technologies.

In addition, the project will engage strategic technical (T2.1) stakeholders (PV plant operators, project

                   101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 17 of 39

development entities, installers, O&M experts, policy-makers, marine associations) and key social actors (T8.1).

1.2.5 NEED FOR THE INTEGRATION OF SOCIAL SCIENCES AND HUMANITIES
SUREWAVE complies with the topic requirement of including SSH issues. On the one hand SUREWAVE will conduct
a specific task for societal engagement (task 8.1) through which a preliminary social analysis will be done of the
future potential massive deployment of the floating offshore PV solution considering social requirements and
needs, spatial planning issues, interaction aspects with local activities (fishing, tourism, local marine industries, etc).
This societal engagement knowledge will be valuated feeding the global solution framework definition (in WP2) to
optimally develop the floating PV solution and potential use-case targeted scenarios and ensure an appropriate
use and perception of the operators with the deployed solutions.
On the other hand, such social aspects will be assessed during the integral sustainability assessment, specifically in
task 7.5 by carrying out a complete Social Lifecycle Assessment (S-LCA) of the SUREWAVE solution and also
including economic analysis and impact of the solution (T7.4) and the interaction between social and economic
aspects (among others) in Task 7.6 when implementing the integrated sustainability assessment.

1.2.6 GENDER DIMENSION
SUREWAVE aims to largely improve in offshore FPV the woman participation rates in comparison with offshore
wind, in which women represent only 21% of the workforce, compared to 32% in renewables overall and 22% in
traditional energy industries like oil and gas30. Based on this, SUREWAVE has considered the gender dimension since
the beginning of the proposal preparation to identify R&D of the project areas in which gender must be analysed
or addressed in a differentiated manner. Based on a preliminary analysis, the following fields will consider an
analysis disaggregated by sex/gender because they might derive in different needs and impact for women and men:

       The development of the SUREWAVE floating offshore PV solution (WP2 to WP6) will consider potential
           combinations of gender & skills differences required at R&D, development and operation level. These
           new technologies, adapted to both sexes, may facilitate in the future the increase of job opportunities in
           a sector that has a huge gender imbalance. These considerations will be analysed within the social
           assessment in WP7.

       Communication and dissemination activities (in WP8) will include the gender dimension in the design of
           the dissemination and communication of the project:
           Use of gender-neutral language and careful selection of visual materials that shows both women and
               men to contribute fighting the traditional stereotype of the traditionally male-dominated tech
               industry.
           Identify and prioritise the participation of women in all the diss & comm and training actions of the
               project.
           Define indicators in the dissemination & communication actions and monitor the gender aspects
               during project execution (ex: workforce gender, audience reached by gender, workers trained (seg. by
               gender)

Finally, all the consortium members are committed to look for equality of opportunities in the project team's
composition and gender dimension will be constantly considered during the project lifetime.

1.2.7 OPEN SCIENCE PRACTICES AS AN INTEGRAL PART OF THE PROPOSED METHODOLOGY
Open science and open innovation are in the DNA of all partners of SUREWAVE. In fact, one of the main pillars of
the project will be the open innovation so all the relevant knowledge actors are included in the project as described
in WP2 to WP7. As indicated before, this is crucial to guarantee the successful progression of the project.

The project aims early and open sharing of research (for example through preregistration, registered reports, pre-
prints, or crowdsourcing) except the potential data that could affect the future commercialization of the whole
offshore floating PV solution and its individual results/services (critical components, materials, tools). All the future
exploitable activities of the research outputs will be done under fair and reasonable conditions. SUREWAVE
partners will commit to rapidly and broadly exploit the project results.

A draft for Data Management plan (DMP) is already available as described in the following section so research data
management will be in included in the research output management. It will be ensured that data that will be
produced in the context of the project and that will not be subjected to commercial exploitation or access
restrictions will be made available as open data, following the FAIR (Findable, Accessible, Interoperable and
Reusable) principle. In particular, open data that can be used by third parties, possibly in different contexts, to

30 Wind energy ­ A gender perspective. International Renewable Energy Agency (IRENA). 2020. https://www.irena.org/publications/2020/Jan/Wind-energy-A-gender-perspective

                                                  101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 18 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

generate new beneficial results, including new open data. In addition, prior to releasing any scientific publication,
the partner involved will contact the Project Management Committee for revision and validation.

SUREWAVE will provide open access (OA) to research outputs (e.g., publications, data, software, models,
algorithms, components´ designs, material mixes, etc) through deposition in trusted repositories. In fact, partners
will provide OA to peer-reviewed scientific publications relating to their results. The authors of all peer-reviewed
scientific publications will choose the most appropriate way of publishing their results, and these publications will
be stored in an OA trusted repository, during and after the project's life following Article 17 and Annex 5 of the GA.
The consortium will be encouraged to publish in the https://open-research-europe.ec.europa.eu/ platform.

1.2.8 DATA MANAGEMENT AND MANAGEMENT OF OTHER RESEARCH OUTPUTS
The project will draw up a first version of the Data Management Plan (DMP) by M6 (D1.3) with definitive update
by M36. SUREWAVE will ensure research data is Findable, Accessible, Interoperable and Reusable (FAIR data
principle) to make possible that knowledge is integrated and available for re-use in future research and projects.
Data sharing will be addressed, taking into consideration any ethical and data protection concerns and fully
complying with privacy issues as ruled by the applicable regulation. The DMP will cover:

       Types of data/research outputs: definition of dataset units to be produced during the project life span.
           Each data set will contain, at least the work package to which it belongs and data typology, with a short
           description.

       Findability of data/research outputs: datasets will be stored in Zenodo, automatically becoming part of
           OpenAIRE, (EC-funded initiative supporting the Open Access policy). Each will be assigned a unique
           identifier.

       Accessibility of data/research outputs: use restrictions and licensing information. Open Access will be
           implemented in peer-review publications, conference proceedings and workshop presentations.

       Interoperability of data/research outputs: to facilitate the use of the data by others and the multi-source
           information combination. Metadata will be open under a Creative Common Public Domain Dedication (CC
           0).

       Reusability of data/research outputs: the right to access and reuse digital research data under the terms
           set out in the Grant Agreement will be ensured. The DMP will include information on licensing of data,
           their availability, re-use of data (duration and reproducibility of research outputs), among other issues.

       Curation and storage/preservation: leader of T1.3 (Data Management) will oversee data management,
           while quality control of each dataset is the responsibility of every WP leader. Informed consent in data
           collection and information protection in data storage and access will be considered, and long-term
           preservation of datasets.

2. IMPACT

2.1 PROJECT'S PATHWAYS TOWARDS IMPACT
2.1.1 CONTRIBUTION OF PROJECT RESULTS TO THE TOPIC OUTCOMES
Topic Outcome 1: Improved overall lifetime, reliability, installability, operability and maintainability of marine
substructures, mechanical joints and energy connection systems for ocean energy devices and/or offshore floating
PV to reduce degradation and failure rates and thus investment risk.
Lifetime: SUREWAVE will improve the overall lifetime not only considering the offshore extreme conditions but
also in comparison with land-locked waters FPV which is below 20 years31. SUREWAVE will put the offshore FPV
lifetime over 20 years achieving the rates of ground mounted solar32, through three main ways:

       1) Robust mechanical connections will be specifically designed in the project and validated by use of
           fatigue testing to ensure reasonable lifetime of these critical parts up to 20 years with low maintenance,
           and flexible enough to avoid critical failure of the components.

       2) The FPV design mainly using corrosion/aging resistant materials (Al/concrete/Polymers) with low
           degradation in offshore conditions (expected durability of the components is around 20 years).

       3) Improved numerical models with reduced uncertainties provides optimal estimate of load and stress
           distribution in the component leading to optimized component design with reduced failure rate (by 10%)
           and improved remaining useful fatigue lifetime by 25% of the different elements of the FPV.

Reliability: Specifically, reliability will be improved by:

31 Goswami, Anik, and Pradip Kumar Sadhu. "Degradation analysis and the impacts on feasibility study of FPV systems." Sustainable Energy, Grids and Networks 26 (2021): 100425
32 Jordan, Dirk C., and Sarah R. Kurtz. "Photovoltaic degradation rates--an analytical review." Progress in photovoltaics: Research and Applications 21.1 (2013): 12-29

                                                     101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 19 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

       1) The solar panel flat-low design directly in contact with water reduces the potential damages (by 70%)
           caused by lifting forces caused by surface wind and Aluminium solar panel shell structure very resistant
           to external loads and un-submersible to maximize availability and generation capacity

       2) Decreased wind and wave exposure (up to 30% decrease intensity) thanks to optimal structural
           attenuating design of the breakwater shape through advanced predictive computational modelling and
           simulation tools (both for the material properties and for the whole structural integrity), and thanks to
           the shielding effect of breaking wave on the solar modules leading to a reduced lifting force, slamming
           load and potential damages.

       3) Concrete breakwater extremely resilient to severe mechanical loads and to physical degradation in
           water environment (corrosion & biofouling).

Installability: It will be drastically reduced thanks to:
       1) The breakwater concept which unlocks the possibility to moor the solar array directly to the
           breakwater. The following assumptions have been made: One tugging operation for a bundle of four
           elevated seaworthy offshore FPV rafts (for instance Moss Maritime or Solar Duck). Assuming 400 Wp solar
           panels. This results in 76 tugging operations for deploying 10 MWp FPV installation. By employing the
           SUREWAVE solution and using a seagoing barge, the number of tugging operations are reduced to 3,
           meaning a 96% reduction of tugging.
       2) easy to mould and port-site casting of the external concrete breakwater thus lowering logistic cost by
           30%.
       3) internal modular and foldable FPV light weight system of PV modules easy to install modular
           breakwater reducing the transportation and logistics cost by 30%.
       4) Energy connection systems easy to install (40% less time) by routing DC homeruns on breakwater,
           reducing need for cable tray floats and allows for combining the operation of mooring and DC homerun
           routing.

O&M: SUREWAVE ensures high-efficient operability and drastic reduction of maintenance tasks thanks to:
       1) ease-to-use-app SHMS providing accurate structural integrity diagnosis reducing maintenance,
           inspection and replacement periods and preventing and reducing the occurrence of critical failures.
       2) improved resilience to mechanical loads and physical marine degradation thanks to novel external
           protective breakwater made of specifically targeted concrete formulation to increase corrosion and
           biofouling resistance.
       3) Smart mechanical connection design enabling quick and easy replacement if critical failure occurs.

The SUREWAVE achievements concerning the reduction of degradation and failure rates as well as the high
reliability and availability of the system energy output will lead to a direct reduction of offshore FPV investment
risk. Besides, the achievements in terms of O&M will lead to reduction of O&M variable and fixed costs (insurance,
etc), thus increasing the credibility and acceptance of the solution by the potential offshore FPV project developers.

Topic Outcome 2: Better understanding of the device's real-life performance allowing a safe reduction in the over-
engineering of devices' specifications
The Structural Health Management System toolkit will serve not only for the O&M of the FPV system in service,
but it will be also conceived to be used as an easy-to-use-app for having an optimal and accurate understanding of
the FPV system real-life behaviour, allowing for the offshore FPV design, sizing, dimensioning, modelling and
simulation to enable the quick, accurate, efficient and easy replication and customization of the offshore FPV
system based in the novel SUREWAVE concept. This SHSM tool will allow to assess the combination of optimal
designs of internal FPV components, mechanical joints, external breakwaters for a specific or a combination of
required offshore conditions not only for worst scenarios but in middle and good scenarios for application in other
sea basins, allowing a safe reduction in the over-engineering of the offshore FPV devices´ specifications.

The SHSM platform will be developed to integrate the novel breakwater solution for offshore extreme conditions
with the internal FPV structures and considering different parametrizable mechanical connections (anchoring,
mooring, internal modules connection) towards an evaluation of the structural health integrity for all the use cases
based on AI. Specifically, this platform would provide a whole framework, which is able to test and validate the
customized models, and to size different components. This simulation platform will focus on compromises
between structural integrity, sustainability and cost-efficiency to optimise durability/cost performances.

Topic Outcome 3: Reduction of LCOE in line with the SET Plan targets (actions should clearly justify estimated LCOE
at project start and end, using a recognised calculation methodology)

SUREWAVE will reduce FPV CAPEX mainly with the circular concrete breakwater concept, and other aspects:

                                                  101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 20 of 39
                                                                                                               Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

        Reduction of design & fabrication costs: 1) internal FPV strings from pressed, rolled marine grade
            Aluminium with reduced material usage and integrating recyclable and pawnable material reducing cost
            by 20% in comparison with current FPV; 2) external breakwater with up to 30% cost reduction owing to
            use of waste aggregates and reduction of 60% of cement; 3) 40% reduction of design time/cost of the
            structural specifications of internal FPV, coupling system and external breakwater including engineering
            of PV field, thanks to accurate insights of the detailed aero and hydrodynamic calculations from the
            simulation toolkit.

        Reduction of the transport & installation costs: 1) individual FPV strings able to be stacked into a common
            shipping container and rapidly deployed into the water, mechanically connected together reducing 30%
            logistic cost; 2) breakwater made of circular concrete mixes allowing cast on port, reducing 30% logistic
            cost; 3) Mooring the array directly to breakwater allowing a reduction of 40% of the anchoring & mooring
            costs.

SUREWAVE will reduce OPEX thanks to the structural integrity assured by the computational modelling toolkit:
        Reduction of failures and maintenance: 1) Avoid 70% of internal FPV system failure thanks to strings
            using corrosion/aging resistant materials 2) Avoid 100% critical failure for fatigue crack of breakwater;
            3) Avoid 80% of critical failure of the mechanical connections and mooring lines thanks to the capacity
            of non-linear wind and (breaking) wave impact loading simulations for different breakwater and PV field
            configurations.
        Reduction of insurance costs: The high reliability on the system thanks to solid combination of the design,
            simulation and in-service predictive O&M provided by the SHMS is expected to reduce insurance costs
            by 20%.
        Reduction of reparation frequency works: Thanks to an accurate prediction of the Maintenance,
            inspection and replacement periods provided by the SHMS a reduction of 30% on the O&M travel
            expenses will be achieved.

SUREWAVE improves the generable energy output of the FPV system offshore thanks to several approaches:
        Reduction of downtimes of the global system: Securing the structural integrity of the system, thanks to
            the SHMS allowing accurate structural design, guarantees at least a reduction of 50% of downtimes in
            operational sea-states and allows for a quick recovery of the availability of the system without critical
            failure during the low frequent survival sea-states (14 m. waves). This means an average increase of 20%
            of the energy output.

 Increasing energy efficiency: 1) increase of 5% in the system efficiency is expected thanks to the internal FPV
    modules flat design maximizing cooling of PV panels limiting diffusion driven processes; 2) potential increase
    of 5% of available time of generation by optimizing the hydrodynamic behaviour minimizing splash zone inside
    FPV thanks to the breakwater attenuating effect on wave-current-wind conditions.

SUREWAVE LCoE calculations: A scenario has been estimated: 1) Location likely to receive high wind and waves
and a substantial solar irradiation outside of the cost of Portugal (39.198900, -9.956500) in Atlantic Sea; 2) Solar
irradiance of 1,645 kWh/m2. (Metonorm 8.0); 3) Wave: wave height > 8m, SIS calculations; 4) Plant size: although
the full-scale preliminary target of SUREWAVE future exploitation is a 50MW plant with 150 W/m2, for this LCoE
calculation a conservative solution is selected (with higher LCoE than the full-scale) of 10MWp, with solar array of
274x274m2 and breakwaters of 300x300 m2 with a conservative power peak of 133 W/m2.

Reference case: Current landlocked waters FPV hypothetically applied for offshore harsh conditions: 1) 15 years
lifetime; 2) 1.1 % annual degradation; 3) (E): The total annual power production calculated is 13,879 MWh/year,
for the first year of operation. 4) CAPEX 1560 /kWp 2 times the costs for regular FPV (0.7833*2=1.57 /W); 5) OPEX
25.4 /kWp/year (2 times typical costs for ground mounted solar 12.7/kWp/year34); 6) Equity discount rate: 8 %;
7) No increasing O&M costs per year. Then LCoE is estimated using PVsyst (referent SW tool for PV systems
analysis) and for the reference case is 161 /MWh which is naturally very high since the base case illustrates an
immature, expensive technology. It is perceived as a high-risk undertaking, hence the high interest rate. O&M costs
are high. The degradation is assumed to be a little higher than what can be expected from a modern ground
mounted PV system, due to immaturity of the technology.

Project case: A simulation has been performed using PVsyst (referent SW tool for PV systems analysis) with the

33 [Global Market Trends and Statistics of Floating Solar Celine Paton, SERIS, NUS 12 October 2021]

more than 5 %, due to the average air and water temperature being below standard testing condition (25°C).

Topic Outcome 4: Contribution to the objectives of the Mission Healthy oceans, seas, coastal and inland waters

SUREWAVE will contribute to "objective 3 - Make the sustainable blue economy carbon-neutral & circular" by:
       Massively reducing GHG emissions from maritime economic activities in the EU thus paving the way
           towards the net zero maritime emissions. In particular, massively increased renewable power could be

           used to provide synthetic fuels to decarbonize navigation, for which conclusive strategies are still lacking.
       Keeping marine ecosystems in balance by designing low-impact constructions and operating conditions

           taking into insights from the environmental assessment on potential local environmental impacts on

           ecosystems.
       Aligned with the Mission, it will be done through the implementation of R&D actions in technical

           solutions for the use of renewable energy as it is the offshore FPV.

The wider impact of SUREWAVE will be achieved by establishing a pathway to the following impacts of the area of
"renewable energy technologies" within the Destination 3 - Sustainable, secure and competitive energy supply:
Availability of disruptive renewable energy and renewable fuel technologies and systems in 2050 in order to
accelerate the replacement of fossil-based energy technologies. The development up to TRL5 of a novel concept of
FPV system able to operate in offshore extreme conditions aims to unlock offshore FPV massive deployment and
maximization of the vast potential of EU seas, thus clearly contributing to the availability of disruptive renewable
energy and to accelerate the replacement of fossil-based energy technologies.
Reduced cost and improved efficiency of renewable energy and renewable fuel technologies and their value chains.
As described above, SUREWAVE will reduce LCoE of Offshore FPV in line with other renewable offshore solutions,
and the advanced predictive computational simulation framework allows for an improvement of costs and
efficiency at design and at O&M level. In addition, the SUREWAVE concept of both the internal FPV components
(T3.1) and the external low-cost circular concrete breakwater (T3.2 and WP4) will be carried out aiming the cost
reduction and improved efficiency of the whole offshore FPV value chain.
De-risking of renewable energy and fuel technologies with a view to their commercial exploitation and net zero
greenhouse gas emissions by 2050. The key goal of SUREWAVE is to overcome the technical and non-technical
challenges that are limiting the growth and feasible operation of offshore FPV. SUREWAVE developments will de-
risk the generation of renewable PV offshore allowing for a large-scale commercial exploitation (as shown in the
exploitation strategy) and contributing to the net zero GHG emissions by 2050 (as shown in impact KPIs).
Better integration of renewable energy and renewable fuel-based solutions in energy consuming sectors. Allowing
for a safe, reliable and cost-efficient operation of the offshore FPV facilitates the local use of renewable energy in
coastal areas thus contributing to a better integration of renewable energies in energy consuming sectors.
Enhanced sustainability of renewable energy and renewable fuels value chains, taking fully into account social,

38 https://www.iea.org/data-and-statistics/charts/annual-direct-co2-emissions-avoided-per-1-gw-of-installed-capacity-by-technology-and-displaced-fuel

                                                  101083342 SUREWAVE­ Part B ­ Page number 24 of 39
                                                                                                              Associated with document Ref. Ares(2022)6486115 - 20/09/2022

economic and environmental aspects in line with the European Green Deal priorities. SUREWAVE will not only
develop an advanced offshore FPV solution, but it will also address the main non-technical barriers and it will be
done involving the relevant actors from technical (SAB in T2.4) and societal (T8.1) point of view, considering social,
economic and environmental aspects achieving an integrated sustainability of the solution (WP7).
More effective market uptake of renewable energy and fuel technologies. The minimization of the investment risk
thanks to the high reliability and the replicability potential on the offshore FPV that SUREWAVE will address, will
directly contribute to an effective market uptake of the solar PV energy in the vast potential of the oceans.

In addition, SUREWAVE will contribute to the following Key Strategic Objectives of Horizon Europe: KSO C, `Making
Europe the first digitally led circular, climate-neutral and sustainable economy through the transformation of its
mobility, energy, construction and production systems´; KSO A, `Promoting an open strategic autonomy by leading
the development of key digital, enabling and emerging technologies, sectors and value chains to accelerate and
steer the digital and green transitions through human-centered technologies and innovations.'

Do not significant harm principle (DNSH): LCA and elements of EIA will be applied (T7.3) to ensure that the
SUREWAVE does not cause significant harm to the environment both into the ocean during installation, O&M and
decommissioning also including the whole life cycle. The project will prioritize the use of low carbon materials for
the FPV and will ensure not to substantially affect marine ecosystems. In addition, the solution is aimed to generate
renewable electricity contributing to climate change mitigation and to reduction of pollution in coastal areas.

Sustainable Development Goals (SDG): SUREWAVE aims to unlock massive deployment of offshore FPV. It is not
only contributing to the scientific impact, but also to the economic and societal impact, addressing the 9 Key Impact
Pathways (KIPs) defined by HORIZON EUROPE and contributing to the following Sustainable Development Goals:
SDG-7. Affordable and Clean Energy, SDG-8. Decent Work and Economic Growth, SDG-9. Industry, Innovation &
Infrastructure, SDG-12. Responsible Consumption and Production and SDG-13. Climate Action.
